# DECISIONS.md

> Running log of ambiguity resolutions, per SPEC.md §0: "If a requirement is ambiguous, choose the simplest interpretation that satisfies the acceptance tests, note the decision in DECISIONS.md, and continue." Newest entries are appended at the bottom.

## Pre-implementation decisions (planning stage)

Each item: the ambiguity → **DECISION** (simplest interpretation) → why it satisfies the acceptance tests (Section 20) and edge cases (Section 14).

1. **Monorepo vs single app packaging.** Spec says "single monorepo, modular folder structure" but also "Next.js — single full-stack app."
   **DECISION:** One pnpm workspace containing one deployable Next.js app; "modules" are internal source folders/packages (`modules/platform`, `modules/wms`) with enforced import boundaries (ESLint rule), not separate services or separate deployments.
   **Why:** No acceptance test requires independent deployment; single app keeps Docker Compose simple and meets the 3G performance budget; boundaries still make CRM/Finance cheap to add later.

2. **Primary keys: UUID vs serial.** Spec never says.
   **DECISION:** UUIDv7 for all PKs; business codes (`GS777`, `YW26-000123`, `YW-001`) are separate unique columns, never PKs. Sequence counters (letters, box seq, batch seq) live in dedicated counter columns/tables updated under row locks.
   **Why:** Edge case 16 requires "codes are labels, FKs are ids"; offline sync already mandates client-generated UUIDs (test 18), so UUIDs unify online/offline creation; UUIDv7 keeps index locality at the 200k-box scale.

3. **`planned` reservation when plan lines are lot-level but boxes are the unit.** Which 50 of 100 boxes get reserved?
   **DECISION:** Reservation is a *count per lot per approved plan* (`available = box_count − Σ reserved counts`). For visibility, the N lowest `seq_in_lot` boxes in `in_stock` are stamped `planned`, but boxes of a lot are **interchangeable at scan time**: scanning any in-stock sibling box counts against the line (reservation swaps to the scanned box). Over-scanning beyond the planned count triggers the `added_on_spot` path.
   **Why:** Test 10 only requires that 50 depart and 50 remain `in_stock`, and that double-planning is prevented — a count-level guard does both. Pinning specific physical boxes would cause false "not on plan" alerts, contradicting test 11's intent and warehouse reality (identical boxes).

4. **Crated boxes' statuses vs crate status.** Do member boxes keep their own status while `crated`?
   **DECISION:** Boxes keep `crate_id` and continue through the normal status machine; `crated` is a location attribute, not a terminal state. A crate scan fans out to all member boxes (one scan_event per box, `method=crate`), and the crate's status/location is derived from its members (they always move together while the crate exists). Crate codes get their own per-WH-per-year sequence (`CR-{WH}{YY}-{00000}`).
   **Why:** Test 19 ("scanning crate at load counts all 18") and 5.5 ("scanning the crate QR = scanning all boxes") work naturally; per-box landed cost (test 16) requires per-box batch membership, which fan-out preserves.

5. **Receipt edit after boxes already loaded/in transit.** 4.4 allows manager edits "after that" with no downstream guard.
   **DECISION:** Structural fields (box_count, dims mode, per-box dims/weight) are locked once any box of the lot has left `in_stock` at the origin WH; only text/photo/attachment fields stay editable (audited). Fixing structural errors after departure goes through the void/deviation paths, not receipt edit.
   **Why:** Prevents retroactive corruption of manifests and cost denominators that tests 13 and 16 depend on; label reconciliation (4.4) only makes sense while boxes are still at origin.

6. **Per-box weight when the lot is `mixed` (boxes have no individual weight) but allocation basis is weight.**
   **DECISION:** A box's kg (and m³) = lot total ÷ box_count — the uniform average — used for allocation, manifests, and capacity gauges. If the optional per-box list was entered, real per-box values win. Chargeable weight of a box derives from these same values.
   **Why:** Test 16's worked example uses per-box kg; averaging makes every box well-defined with zero extra operator input (edge case 8: "can't measure each"), and sums remain exactly the lot totals, so batch denominators stay correct.

7. **`direct_to_client` allocation basis semantics.**
   **DECISION:** The cost entry carries a required `client_id`; the amount is distributed only over that client's boxes within the entry's scope (receipt or batch), proportionally by weight. If the client has no boxes in scope, the entry is rejected at save time.
   **Why:** Keeps the invariant "box landed cost = Σ shares" (6.9) — Finance later needs everything at box level; weight is the spec's stated default basis, so no new concept is introduced.

8. **Letter preview race.** Wizard shows "≈ D, E, F" but another operator may confirm first.
   **DECISION:** Preview is a non-binding estimate (already marked "≈"); no letters are reserved at draft time; actual assignment happens only inside the confirm transaction with the warehouse row locked. If assigned letters differ from the preview, the confirm result screen simply shows the real ones.
   **Why:** Test 4 (concurrent confirms get disjoint letters) is satisfied by the transactional assignment alone; reserving at draft time would leak/park letters and violate 5.3's immutability rule.

9. **Batch code for quick batches (AND→TAS, TAS1→TAS2).**
   **DECISION:** Same per-origin-WH sequence and format as any batch (e.g., `AND-003`, `TAS1-007`). A quick batch auto-creates a load plan with a single auto-`approved` version (no agent loop), so loading mode, manifest, deviations, and costing reuse the same code path.
   **Why:** 6.8 says internal moves are "normal transfer batches"; one uniform pipeline means tests 10–14 and the allocation engine (16) apply unchanged to UZ-side moves.

10. **Timezone for the "same warehouse-day" edit rule.**
    **DECISION:** The creator's free-edit window ends at midnight in the *receipt's warehouse* timezone, comparing the warehouse-local calendar date of `created_at` to the warehouse-local date of "now." (Not the editor's locale, not UTC, not a rolling 24 h.)
    **Why:** Section 3 already mandates WH-local display/print time; this matches the operator's shift reality and is deterministic for the audit trail (test 17).

11. **Session storage.**
    **DECISION:** Opaque session IDs in a Postgres `sessions` table (user, device label, UA, IP, created/last-seen, revoked), referenced by the httpOnly cookie. No Redis, no JWT for normal auth.
    **Why:** 4.1 requires a device list and "logout other devices," which needs server-side session state; spec explicitly avoids Redis; JWT stays reserved for the print helper only.

12. **What `received` vs `in_stock` mean practically.**
    **DECISION:** `received` is not a stored box status — the `boxes` status set starts at `in_stock`: receipt confirmation creates boxes directly as `in_stock`, and the single confirm movement row in `box_movements` represents spec 5.5's `received → in_stock` arrow. Operationally, a confirmed box is immediately available for planning/crating. (Aligned with the schema in ARCHITECTURE.md — one source of truth.)
    **Why:** No test or workflow ever acts on a lingering `received` state; keeping the arrow in the movement history honors 5.5 without inventing an extra manual step for the operator (3-minute budget, 6.1).

13. **Label reprint for unclaimed → assigned cargo.**
    **DECISION:** Box short codes (QR payloads) never change on assignment. The assign-to-client flow ends with a "Reprint N labels" button generating labels with the new client code + existing letters; old `#UNKNOWN` labels are flagged "to be replaced/destroyed" in the same reconciliation UI as 4.4; physically re-stickering is the operator's manual act. Unclaimed lots receive letters normally at confirm (label shows `#UNKNOWN` + receipt number in the client-code slot).
    **Why:** Test 9 requires "labels reprintable with new code" with both actors audited; stable QR payloads mean already-applied stickers still scan correctly even if not replaced.

14. **FX rate selection "on the cost date."**
    **DECISION:** Use the latest rate whose date ≤ the cost entry's date for the (currency → USD) pair; if none exists, saving the entry prompts the user to enter today's rate first (no silent fallback). Rate edits trigger the idempotent recompute job.
    **Why:** Test 16 needs "each entry's dated rate" to be deterministic; edge cases 17–18 (late costs, corrected rates) are covered by the recompute path.

15. **Cost allocation denominator and membership changes.**
    **DECISION:** A batch entry's denominator = Σ (basis metric) of boxes *actually on the batch* per the reconciled manifest (including `added_on_spot`, excluding `short_loaded`). Any later membership change (late scan, missing-in-transit resolved, undocumented transfer) re-triggers the recompute job for affected batches.
    **Why:** This is the only reading under which test 16's arithmetic (10,000 CNY / 10,000 kg actually loaded) and test 13/14 resolutions stay mutually consistent.

16. **`ready_for_pickup` at AND when cargo continues to Tashkent.** Section 1 says pickup happens "after customs clearance"; 6.8 says boxes become `ready_for_pickup` right after unload at any customs/distribution WH.
    **DECISION:** Follow 6.8 literally: unload at a `customs`/`distribution` WH sets `ready_for_pickup` for all clients' boxes. Planning boxes into an onward quick batch transitions them `ready_for_pickup → planned/loading` (allowed extra arrow). Customs clearance itself is not modeled in Phase 1.
    **Why:** 6.8 is the operative workflow section; test 20 (partial pickup) and the notification rules (Section 11) key off `ready_for_pickup` at unload; modeling clearance would add a state no test exercises.

17. **Density badge boundaries.** "🟠 300–400, 🟢 200–300" leaves ties ambiguous.
    **DECISION:** Lower-bound inclusive intervals: blue < 200, green [200, 300), orange [300, 400), red ≥ 400.
    **Why:** Test 5 fixes the only tested tie: density exactly 200 → 🟢.

18. **Receipt-number and box-code sequence scopes.**
    **DECISION:** Receipt seq resets per WH per warehouse-local day (matching the date in the number). Box short-code sequence is per WH per year (`YY` in the code), rolling over at WH-local Jan 1; batch seq per origin WH, never resets. All counters use `SELECT … FOR UPDATE` like the letter sequencer.
    **Why:** Mirrors the formats in 5.2 exactly; the concurrency guarantee of test 4 is reused for every counter for free.

19. **Loading a crate whose member lots are only partially on the plan.**
    **DECISION:** Crate scan evaluates each member box against the plan individually: members whose lot line still has remaining planned count are counted; members beyond plan go through one aggregated `added_on_spot` confirmation (single reason for the whole crate).
    **Why:** Keeps test 11 and test 19 simultaneously true without forcing the logist to pre-model crates in plans.

20. **Offline receipt-draft identity.**
    **DECISION:** Drafts and scan events both carry client-generated UUIDs used as idempotency keys on sync; a draft syncs as `draft` status and is only ever *confirmed* online (letter assignment requires the DB transaction). Offline confirm is not supported — the wizard queues the draft and prompts to confirm when back online.
    **Why:** Test 18 requires dedupe; 5.3 makes letter assignment inherently online-transactional, and 6.1 only requires drafts to survive connection loss, not offline confirmation.



## Completeness-review addendum (pre-implementation)

A cross-check of the draft plan against every spec section surfaced these additional resolutions:

21. **Seed timing vs spec §18 ("must ship with M1").** The batch seed (`YW-001` departed, `KA-001` in approval loop) and cost/FX seed require M3/M6 schema. **DECISION:** the seed script grows with each milestone — warehouses/users/clients at M0, the canonical receipts at M1, batches + presets at M3, cost entries + FX at M6 — so each §18 item ships with the first milestone whose schema supports it.

22. **Batch status set.** Spec §6.5 uses `unloaded`/`closed`, the kanban (§10) uses Arrived/Closed. **DECISION:** statuses are `forming, loading, in_transit, arrived, unloaded, closed, cancelled`; `arrived` = truck at destination, `unloaded` = finish-unload reconciliation done; the kanban "Arrived" column shows both `arrived` and `unloaded`.

23. **Currencies are a table, not a hardcoded list.** `currencies` (`CNY`,`USD`,`UZS` seeded, admin-extensible per §4.6); cost-entry UI defaults to CNY at `country='CN'` warehouses (§6.1).

24. **Offline scanning scope.** The IndexedDB outbox built in M3 for Loading mode is a shared primitive reused verbatim by Unload (M4) and Issue (M5) modes — §15's "all scan modes fully functional offline" is satisfied by reuse, not per-mode reimplementation.

## M0 implementation decisions

25. **Translation provider (owner's answer, 2026-07-22).** Owner chose a free/open translation API for now, to be swapped for a paid provider later. **DECISION:** `translation_provider` defaults to `libretranslate` (self-hostable/open); the provider interface stays pluggable per spec §8, so switching to Yandex/DeepL is a settings + API-key change. Wired up in M1 with the product dictionary.
26. **Telegram bot token** provided by the owner; stored only in `.env` (gitignored) as `TELEGRAM_BOT_TOKEN`, never committed. Bot integration itself lands in M1 per the plan.
27. **UI fonts = system font stack** (no webfont files at all): zero font bytes on 3G, inherently China-safe, and CJK renders via the OS. Label PDFs will embed a self-hosted CJK font separately in M1 (pdf-lib needs a real font file).
28. **Local-disk storage driver for dev** behind the same `StorageDriver` interface as S3/MinIO, with HMAC-signed serve URLs so the signed-URL access model (spec 4.8) holds in dev too. `STORAGE_DRIVER=s3` in production compose.
29. **Audit immutability enforced twice**: `REVOKE UPDATE/DELETE` (covers the non-superuser app role in production) **plus** a `BEFORE UPDATE OR DELETE` trigger raising an exception — the trigger also protects dev databases where the app connects as a superuser.
30. **pg-boss runs in-process** with the Next.js server, started from the `instrumentation` hook — matches the single-app Docker topology (spec §3); a separate worker container can be split out later without code changes.
31. **Password hashing via `@node-rs/argon2`** (prebuilt native binding) with OWASP Argon2id parameters — the `argon2` npm package needs node-gyp toolchains that complicate CI/Docker.
32. **`settings` audit rows** use the fixed entity id `00000000-0000-0000-0000-000000000001` (audit_log.entity_id is `uuid NOT NULL`; settings keys are strings — the key lives in the diff payload).

## M1 implementation decisions

33. **Client-generated receipt/lot UUIDs.** The wizard mints receipt and lot ids client-side: photos upload against the lot id *before* confirm (no orphan-attachment repointing), and the receipt id doubles as the idempotency key — a double-tap or offline retry returns the already-confirmed receipt instead of duplicating it.
34. **Draft autosave = localStorage (M1).** Wizard text state persists to localStorage after every change and survives app kill; photos upload immediately when online. The full IndexedDB outbox (photos offline, scan events) arrives with the M3 scanner per the plan — recorded as the M1 slice of spec §15 offline.
35. **Letter preview is non-binding** (spec DECISIONS #8 confirmed in code): shown as "≈ D, E, F"; real assignment only inside the confirm transaction.
36. **Seed letters mirror the owner's canonical example**: fixed UUIDv7-format ids make the M1 seed idempotent; GS777 → A/B/C and GS102 → D are created through the real `confirmReceipt` service, so seed data exercises the production code path.
37. **Label CJK font**: Noto Sans SC subset-embedded via pdf-lib+fontkit (~50 KB per PDF). Verified with pdf.js (the Chrome/Android renderer family); poppler warns on the CFF subset but that does not affect RawBT/browser printing. Latin text uses built-in Helvetica (codes stay crisp at 203 dpi).
38. **Telegram delivery model**: notification rows are per-(user, channel); the in-app bell row is written immediately, the Telegram row is sent by a pg-boss worker with retry/backoff — delivery failures stay visible on the row (admin view arrives in M6 polish).

## M1.5 decisions (owner feedback round, 2026-07-23)

39. **Client code format**: any 2–10 uppercase alphanumerics — the owner's real Kashgar stock file uses 444, 555, 111, GS277. `client_code_prefix` now only drives auto-generation. (Owner asked; answered from their own data.)
40. **Multiple codes per person**: interim decision — each code remains its own client card (letters, stock, notifications per code); a person-level grouping arrives with CRM (Phase 2). Owner to confirm.
41. **Unclaimed marking**: unknown-code cargo stores the marking written on the boxes (`receipts.unclaimed_marking`); labels print `MARKING-letter`; assignment to a client clears it and re-notifies the sales manager. This also enables "create the client from the marking later".
42. **Single-window receiving** replaces the stepper (owner's request) — same data, same validation, one screen; draft autosave unchanged.
43. **Edit-after-confirm proxy rule**: "manager-level" = holders of `receipts.void` (managers/logists/admins per §16); operators hold `receipts.edit` but are restricted to the same warehouse-local day as creator.

## M1.6 decisions (owner feedback round 2, 2026-07-23)

44. **"Pieces" (sht) field replaced with a free-text note per line.** The owner's real Kashgar file concatenates a remark into the product-name cell (e.g. "loader miscounted during loading"); modeling that as a dedicated `note` column is more faithful and more useful than a numeric pieces count nobody asked to keep. `pieces_count`/`packaging_type` columns were dropped (not deprecated) since no production data existed yet.
45. **Receipt-level attachments are a separate concept from per-lot box photos.** Per-lot photos (`entityType='receipt_lot'`) remain the mandatory "box appearance" proof required to confirm; receipt-level attachments (`entityType='receipt'`) are optional supporting documents (invoice, packing list, anything) shared across the whole receipt. Reused the generic `attachments` table and upload API — only the entityId differs.
46. **Responsive strategy: two renderings, one state.** Rather than one layout awkwardly serving both device classes, the product-line editor renders twice — a real `<table>` on desktop (`hidden md:block`) and the original stacked cards on mobile (`md:hidden`) — sharing the same `draft` state and update functions. Both are always in the DOM; CSS `display` toggles which one is visible. This is why e2e locators must scope to the visible container (`#mobile-product-lines`) rather than assume a single match.
47. **Desktop table keeps the mode toggle inline** rather than splitting into separate uniform/mixed table schemas — a single "dims" cell swaps its inputs, keeping column count stable and Excel-like.

## Post-M1.6 feedback-round decisions (2026-07-23)

48. **Per-lot note removed from the intake UI** (amends #44): the owner decided line-level remarks are not needed while receiving. The `receipt_lots.note` column stays — it remains editable post-confirm via the receipt-detail lot editor and visible in the stock table; only the entry screen dropped it. The wizard no longer submits a note at confirm time.
49. **Russian product name is display-only at intake**: shown in parentheses under the Chinese name, filled by the dictionary/translation pipeline; the manual ru input was removed from the receiving screen (owner's request). Corrections happen via the product dictionary admin or the post-confirm lot editor.
50. **Intake cost capture = one total amount + currency**, stored as a single `cost_entries` row under the seeded `other` cost type (owner: "bir qabulda ikki-uchta narsaga alohida kirgizish shart emas"). Consequence for M6 reporting: receiving-time costs all land under "other" unless a reclassification/typed-entry UI is added later (noted in PLAN M6).
51. **"General box photos" are a first-class receipt-level concept** (amends #45): photos of the overall boxes upload with `entityType='receipt'`/kind `photo`, get their own camera button at intake, and surface in the stock list next to the product photo with an amber border. Receipt-level *file* attachments (invoice, packing list) remain a separate button in the same row.
52. **Local-driver attachment reads stream bytes directly** (amends #28): `/api/attachments/[id]` now returns the file with the correct Content-Type instead of 302-redirecting to a signed URL. The redirect built an absolute URL whose host could differ from the one the browser used — a phone on LAN Wi-Fi was sent to `localhost` and every image broke. S3 deployments keep the presigned-URL redirect.
53. **`LightboxImg` is the standard photo thumbnail**: every photo thumb (receiving, stock, attachments) opens a tap-to-zoom overlay in place — never a navigation or new tab (owner: photos must open "alohida oynachada"). Falls back to the original variant if a thumbnail is missing.
54. **No photo-required indicator on product lines** (reverses the round-3 icon): the owner wants no badge on/near photos; the disabled Confirm button is the only signal that a line still needs its mandatory photo.
55. **Dual rendering now applies only to the product-line editor** (amends #46): the client/warehouse header and the note/cost/files panel render once and are shared by both layouts; only the lot editor keeps the desktop-table + mobile-cards split, so e2e locators scope to `#mobile-product-lines` for line inputs only.

## M2 decisions (2026-07-23, owner's answers)

56. **Crating cost targets the crate itself** (owner Q1): `cost_entries` gained scope `crate` + `crate_id`; the entry also carries the crate's `client_id` so the M6 allocation engine can attribute it even when the crate spans several receipts of that client.
57. **Return-to-sender is whole-receipt** (owner Q2): the entire unclaimed receipt is handed over at once; the receiver's name + phone are mandatory, photo optional. `handovers` is a general handover table — the M5 client-issue flow will reuse it with kind `issued_to_client`.
58. **A lost box is recoverable** (owner Q3): manager-level `lost → in_stock` ("found") with a mandatory reason, audited; `void` remains terminal. Boxes inside an active crate cannot be marked lost/void — dissolve first, so crate contents stay truthful.
59. **Wrong-WH move guard** (recommended default; owner asked for a re-explanation of Q4 — to revisit if he disagrees): a receipt moves only while every box is `in_stock` and un-crated; the receipt number and letters keep the origin-WH prefix (immutable labels) and the UI prompts a label reprint after the move.
60. **Daily digest is one consolidated message** (owner Q5): 09:00 Asia/Tashkent (04:00 UTC pg-boss cron), sections per warehouse, sent to logist + admins over the existing notification rows/Telegram worker; suppressed entirely when there is nothing to report.
61. **Stock XLSX is download-only** (owner Q6): ad-hoc filtered exports are not archived as attachments (they'd pile up with no owning entity); official archived reports arrive with M6. Every export is still audit-logged.
62. **Manager gates for M2 ops** (extends #43): box lost/void/found and receipt WH-move require `receipts.void`; crate build/dissolve/label requires `crates.manage`; return-to-sender requires `receipts.unclaimed.resolve`.

## M3 decisions (2026-07-23, owner's answers)

63. **The agent never enters the system** (owner Q1): the logist downloads the plan Excel, sends it to the agent over Telegram/WhatsApp, and records the verdict (approved / changes_requested + comment) on the plan version. Versions are immutable; a resubmit after changes creates v(N+1).
64. **No server-side plan drafts**: the editor's working selection lives client-side; the first "send to agent" creates the plan directly in `pending_agent` (the schema's `draft` status stays reserved). Cheaper than draft persistence and matches how the logist actually works — build, send, done.
65. **Scan input = camera AND HID** (owner Q2): native `BarcodeDetector` when the browser has it, `@zxing/browser` fallback otherwise, plus a global keystroke listener for USB/Bluetooth HID scanners (they type the code + Enter). One `Scanner` component serves all future scan modes.
66. **Partial-lot plans don't pin boxes** (owner Q3): approval reserves the N lowest-`seq_in_lot` boxes as `planned`, but whichever boxes the loader actually scans are what departs — a scan of an unplanned box of the SAME batch-reserved lot still goes through the not-on-plan confirm, keeping the fact trail honest.
67. **Offline scans**: IndexedDB outbox with client-generated event UUIDs; `/api/scan/sync` acks per item and the server dedupes on the UUID (`ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`); crate fan-out rows get `uuid5(boxId, eventUuid)` derived ids. The loading screen validates against a cached batch snapshot for <300 ms local feedback.
68. **SSE deferred**: the loading counter is local-first and the logist sees state on refresh; the `/api/sse` live-push layer (plan live-adds, remote progress) moves to the M4/M5 polish backlog — recorded as a deviation from the original M3 task list.
69. **Sticker-lost = manual entry from the unscanned list** (edge case 3): the loading screen lists the batch's unscanned boxes; tapping one (or typing its code) records a `manual` scan with reason `sticker_lost`. The reprint offer arrives with the M4 polish.
70. **Truck presets are seed-managed for now**: two real presets seeded (13.6 m / 90 m³ / 24 t and 17.5 m / 130 m³ / 28 t); a presets admin CRUD is deferred until the owner asks.

## M4 decisions (2026-07-23)

71. **Unload has no confirm dialog for off-manifest scans** (edge case 4, "reality wins"): a known box scanned at unload is ALWAYS accepted — on-manifest lands normally, off-manifest is auto-transferred to this warehouse with flag `undocumented_transfer`, a correcting movement, and an instant logist Telegram. The loader never decides; the system records reality and alerts.
72. **Unknown QR at unload links to the full receiving screen** rather than a separate mini-intake form — the receive screen is already single-window and fast; a second intake form would drift from it. The ack toast carries the link.
73. **First unload scan flips the batch to `arrived`** (no separate "truck arrived" button); `finishUnload` flags never-scanned manifest boxes `missing_in_transit` (they stay `in_transit` on the batch) and the resolutions are `found_at_origin` (back to origin stock) / `found_here` (into destination stock), manager-gated per #43.
74. **KA hub dashboard v1 = the /transit report**: in-transit/arrived batches + all missing-in-transit boxes in one screen; the fuller role dashboard with charts arrives in M6 as planned.
75. **Batch-scope cost entry UI deferred to M6** (with the FX/cost module) — the schema already accepts scope=batch entries on closed batches, so nothing blocks late cost capture when that UI lands.

## M5 decisions (2026-07-23)

76. **VED invoice/packing list are download-drafts, not in-app documents**: rows come from the actual manifest; the price column is blank with a live amount formula — the VED manager fills prices in Excel before sending (in-app price editing deferred until asked). The company header comes from settings keys `company_name/address/phone` — **owner still owes the real requisites (open Q4)**.
77. **`handovers` generalized** (migration 0009): `receipt_id` nullable + `client_id`/`debt_ok` added — one table for whole-receipt returns (M2) and client issues (M5), matching the original architecture sketch.
78. **Issue flow is one idempotent submit, not per-scan events**: the phone selects boxes locally (tap or scan against a local list — works offline until the final confirm), then a single `issueBoxes` call with a client-generated handoverId creates the handover, flips boxes to `issued`, writes `issue` scan_events, and notifies the sales manager with the remaining count.
79. **Client-notify "Share" = message drafts inside the manager's Telegram**: the ReadyForPickup notification carries ready-to-forward client texts in uz AND ru (owner's Q5 wording: arrived, being cleared) — no separate share UI.
80. **Quick batches are plan-less batches**: created directly from the batch board (manager gate `batches.depart_close`); the load scanner detects the absent plan and accepts any loose box at the origin (in_stock or ready_for_pickup) without the not-on-plan ceremony or flags.
81. **`ready_for_pickup` happens at customs/distribution unload** and those boxes stay issuable and quick-batch-loadable; plan approval marks batches to customs warehouses as `export`.
82. **Unclaimed labels print the box marking** (feedback round 5): the sticker's dominant text is `MARKING-A` (what is physically written on the cargo) with a small `#UNKNOWN` flag beneath — `#UNKNOWN` alone only when there is no marking. The renderer no longer overrides the code the route computed.
83. **A crate is one place in a plan** (migration 0011): `load_plan_lines.crate_id` — crated boxes leave loose availability, the editor offers whole crates as single ticks (place count = loose boxes + crates), and approval reserves the crate's exact boxes so loading the crate QR never trips not-on-plan.
84. **Truck presets are owner-managed** (`/trucks`, `plans.manage` gate): create/edit/hide with audit; the plan editor keeps a ⚙️ link next to the truck select. Hidden presets keep history but leave the dropdown.
85. **Vehicle info stays editable until the batch closes** — a wrong plate/driver is fixable after departure; the read-only card only appears on closed/cancelled batches.
86. **`fx_rates` is USD-base, per-currency, dated** (migration 0012): the M0 pair-based placeholder (from/to) was never written and got replaced wholesale. `rate_to_usd` = USD per 1 unit; lookup takes the latest rate ≤ the cost date (falls back to the earliest known); a currency with no rates leaves the entry unconverted and visibly flagged instead of guessing.
87. **Allocations are materialized** (`cost_allocations`, rebuilt whole per entry by an idempotent job): batch-scope shares distribute over the boxes that actually DEPARTED on the batch (current members before departure); recompute fires on entry create/void, FX edit (per currency) and batch depart. A box's landed cost = Σ its allocation rows.
88. **Share rounding**: 4 decimal places; the last box absorbs the rounding drift so shares always sum exactly to the entry's USD amount (keeps report totals honest).
89. **Invoice layout comes from the owner's real ka23 file** (feedback round 6): one combined "INVOICE & PACKING LIST" sheet — Invoice №/date/container, Sender/Seller/Consignee + transport/delivery/customs-post requisites stored as editable settings (`ved_*`, defaults lifted from the uploaded file), table with ТНВЭД column; prices+ТНВЭД left for the VED manager, measured weight prefills both netto and brutto, amount/totals are live formulas.
90. **Warehouse fill thresholds are 60%/80%** (owner's numbers): optional `capacity_m3` per warehouse; home shows occupied m³ (in_stock/planned/loading/ready) vs capacity — yellow from 60%, red + 🚨 from 80% ("time to ship"). Warehouse-scoped staff see their own warehouses only.
91. **Backups: local disk for now** (owner's answer, off-site revisited later): nightly 02:00 Tashkent pg_dump -Fc to BACKUP_DIR (.data/backups), 30-day retention, same code as `pnpm backup`; failure is logged loudly, restore via pg_restore documented in the module.
92. **Plan editor empties the stock table on origin change** (flake root cause): the old origin's rows stayed visible while the new fetch ran, so counts typed there vanished when fresh data landed; now the list clears synchronously and in-flight fetches abort.
93. **Inventory runs parallel, reality wins, manager decides** (owner's answers): no operations freeze during stocktake; a box scanned here but recorded elsewhere moves here on submit (`inventory_found` correcting movement — issued/void boxes never auto-move, they're listed as skipped); unscanned boxes become `lost` ONLY when ticked by someone holding `receipts.void` (warehouse manager+), and the whole summary lands in the admins'/logists' Telegram (`InventoryCompleted`).
94. **Aging colors are 7/14 days** (owner's numbers): stock/aging report shows days yellow from 7 and red from 14; the digest threshold (`stale_stock_days`) stays a separate admin setting.
95. **Landed cost is management-only** (owner's answer): the report and box-card breakdown stay behind `reports.all_warehouses` / cost permissions — sales managers do not see cost data.
96. **All nine §13 reports ship with server-side sorting** (M6 #13): sortable headers write ?sort/&dir to the URL so ordering composes with filters and survives reloads; sorting happens in the page over the already-scoped rows (report row counts are bounded — 300–500). Management-only reports (landed cost, client history, staff activity, label prints) sit behind `reports.all_warehouses`; the rest also serve warehouse-scoped staff.
97. **Cost-hygiene warning on the dashboard**: batches departed more than 3 days ago with zero non-voided cost entries surface in the attention card (spec 6.9's unclaimed-cost warning) — visible to all-warehouse report holders.
98. **No segment-level `loading.tsx` under `(protected)` (Next 15.1 quirk)**: adding a root loading boundary made the client router abort every RSC fetch — link navigation degraded to full page loads (invisible) but `redirect()` from server actions and `router.refresh()` silently did nothing (crate dissolve stayed on the page, box lost/found never updated) while the DB commit went through. Bisected to the single file; removed. Revisit skeletons after a Next.js upgrade, per-page if ever.
99. **UX audit conventions** (part 6): detail pages always carry a "← back to list" link (`BackLink`); lists show a localized "empty" line instead of "—"; destructive buttons on data-bearing things confirm first; every async button shows a pending state; icon-only touch targets are at least 28 px.
100. **Per-user mutes are Telegram-only and grouped** (spec §11 "per-user mute settings"): stored on `users.muted_notification_types` as a jsonb list of event types (`'all'` = everything), but the profile UI exposes three human groups — daily digest / alerts (off-plan, missing, undocumented) / operational messages — plus a master "mute all". The in-app bell is never muted (spec: it mirrors everything), and the weekly `RestoreTestFailed` alert deliberately bypasses mutes — a broken backup must always reach an admin.
101. **Weekly restore fire drill + measured perf pass**: `db.restore_test` (Sun 04:00 Tashkent, after the night backup) restores the newest dump into scratch DB `gsr_restore_test`, requires users/warehouses/clients/receipts/boxes/batches to come back non-degenerate, drops the scratch, and pings admins' Telegram on failure; `pnpm restore-test` is the manual twin (verified against a real dump). Perf: first-load JS is 104 kB shared / ≤150 kB on the worst route — within the 3G budget — so M6-8's "recharts dashboards" stays CSS-only bars (no chart lib, no CDN) and list virtualization is skipped: every list is already server-limited/paginated.
102. **Deploy path hardened for the owner's "probniy server" ask**: compose gained a one-shot `migrate` service (build-stage image runs `db:migrate && db:seed` before the app starts — the slim runtime image can't) and an optional `caddy` profile (automatic HTTPS, required for the phone camera scanner); `.dockerignore` added so `.env`/`.data` never enter the image; `ops/bootstrap.sh` turns a fresh Ubuntu VPS into a running stack in one command; owner steps documented in docs/DEPLOY.md. Claude cannot buy/create a public server itself (account+payment) — the owner's part is capped at ~10 minutes, or they can hand over SSH access and Claude does the rest.
103. **PDF CJK text goes through a HarfBuzz subset, never fontkit's** (owner's "no product name on stickers" bug): pdf-lib/fontkit `subset: true` produced invalid embedded fonts for Noto Sans SC — CFF and converted-TTF alike — so viewers/printers silently dropped the glyphs. Every PDF (box/crate labels, handover act) now builds its font with `subset-font` (HarfBuzz wasm) over the document's actual characters + printable ASCII + full Cyrillic, embedded with `subset: false`. The font asset itself became a TTF (converted from the OTF with fontTools) and `subset-font`/`harfbuzzjs` joined the webpack server-externals list. Label PDF: correct 化妆品 (Косметика) at ~9 KB/page.
104. **Departure is a loader's action too** (owner, round 8): `departBatchAction` accepts `batches.depart_close` OR `scan.load` scoped to the ORIGIN warehouse — the person who physically finished loading may send the truck; the UI always asks for confirmation since departure locks the load. Close/arrival remains `batches.depart_close` only.
105. **"Stock" means physically-on-the-shelf** (owner's TAS1 report): the stock browser and stock XLSX include `in_stock`, `planned`, `loading` and `ready_for_pickup` — matching the dashboard's IN_WAREHOUSE set — with each box's exact status shown. Only in-transit/issued/lost/void boxes leave the stock view.
106. **Scan feedback is sound-first on iOS**: `navigator.vibrate` doesn't exist there, so a WebAudio beep (armed by the first touch, as iOS demands) accompanies vibration on all four scan screens; and each screen's periodic outbox flush now re-fetches the batch snapshot, union-merging server state so multi-phone scanning counts live without a page refresh.
107. **ТНВЭД assistant is memory-first and human-confirmed** (Phase 1.5, owner's spec): `tnved_assignments` keys on the normalized zh product name; a stored code pre-fills the batch ТНВЭД page and the ka23 invoice column without any AI call. The AI (Claude, `claude-opus-4-8`, photo + name, structured JSON output) is consulted only for unknown products, prompted to pick a duty-optimal but customs-DEFENSIBLE code with explicit confidence; its answer is a draft until the VED manager saves it (source 'ai' vs 'manual' recorded, saves audited). No `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` → manual entry keeps working and the button says exactly what to configure.
108. **Client ledger has no tariffs and freezes FX at entry** (Phase 2.1, owner: "hech qanday tarif yo'q — sotuvchi va klient narxni birga kelishishadi"): `client_transactions` records only two things — charges (the negotiated price, usually entered on the batch pricing page by the VED manager/accountant after customs) and payments (cash/card/transfer in any currency). Unlike cost entries (which recompute when FX changes), a ledger row stores `rate_to_usd`/`amount_usd` at entry time and never moves — settled money must not drift. A currency with no FX rate at all is rejected (`fx_missing`) instead of leaving an unconvertible hole in the balance; mistakes are voided with a reason (audited, struck-through), never deleted.
109. **Debt gate is permission-guarded, not a checkbox for everyone** (owner: "manager ruxsati bilan yuklar beriladi"): `issueBoxes` blocks when the client's USD balance > 0 unless `debtOk` is set, and the action layer only accepts `debtOk` from holders of the new `finance.debt_override` (warehouse manager, sales manager, accountant, logist, admins — NOT warehouse operators). The M5-era `handovers.debt_ok` column finally carries logic; the issue screen shows the debt before scanning starts so the operator calls the manager early, and the override lands in the handover + audit trail.
110. **Client cabinet lives inside the staff bot, uz-only, resolved per request** (Phase 2.2): one `TELEGRAM_BOT_TOKEN` serves both audiences — `/start <code>` first tries the staff `telegram_links` table, then `client_telegram_links` (codes are single-use and minted only by staff holding `clients.manage`, from the admin client page). Cabinet texts are hardcoded Uzbek (the clients' language) rather than next-intl — there is no per-chat locale. Every button/callback re-resolves the chat's linked clients and re-checks lot ownership (callback data is untrusted), so a revoked link cuts access on the next tap. One chat may represent several clients (brokers) and photos are sent from the 800px server thumbnails, not originals. Ready/issued client pings are direct best-effort sends in the events worker (logged, never retried, never block staff notifications) — the cabinet always shows the same state on demand.
111. **Cabinet links are not bearer tokens — identity is phone-verified** (owner's incident: client A's link reached person B, who saw A's cargo/debt): `/start <code>` now only starts verification; the bot demands the tapper's OWN phone via Telegram's contact button (contact.user_id must equal the sender — a forwarded contact card is an impersonation attempt), and completes the link only when the number matches one of the client card's phones (`phonesMatch`: digits-only, last-9 suffix comparison, so formatting and country codes never false-negative). Mismatch burns the code, replies neutrally with zero client data, and alerts the staff creator in Telegram; a client with no phone on file can't link at all until staff adds the number (code kept for retry). Consequence documented for brokers: their phone must be added to the client's phones list first.
112. **The phone, not the code, is the cabinet identity** (owner: one person holds 2–4 marking codes on one number, and re-linking per code annoyed him into asking for the old unverified flow back): verification stays (it is what caught incident #111), but succeeds ONCE per person — after the contact check, `linkAllClientsForPhone` attaches every active client whose card carries that number, and `autoLinkClientToVerifiedChats` (called from client create/update) attaches newly opened codes to already-verified chats with a bot ping. Consequence: staff keeps client phones accurate — the phone field now drives both security and convenience. Client create/update also redirect to the client CARD so the auto-assigned code is immediately visible.
113. **The tracking map is a self-drawn SVG simulation, never external tiles** (owner's "GPS-like" ask): OpenStreetMap/Google tiles would be slow-to-dead behind the GFW for the China warehouses and would add an external dependency, so the corridor (GZ/YW→Urumqi→Kashgar→Irkeshtam→Osh→AND/TAS) is hand-projected into a 1000×600 viewBox (x=(lon−66)·16.6, y=(45−lat)·24) and shipped inline. Positions come from `estimateTransit` over per-corridor segment schedules built from the owner's numbers (border wait modeled as a stationary 24–72 h segment); the known drift ("sometimes the truck waits longer") is corrected by one-tap checkpoint pins on the batch card (`batches.tracking_checkpoint`, audited) that re-anchor the clock, and past-schedule trucks turn red ("overdue — call the driver") instead of pretending to know. Unknown warehouse pairs fall back to a straight line with generic timing; warehouses absent from the drawing simply don't plot.
114. **The real basemap is still self-hosted — external providers lost on their own turf** (owner asked for Yandex/Baidu/etc.): Baidu Maps requires a mainland-ID developer account (the owner cannot obtain one) and its Uzbekistan coverage is unusable; Yandex JS API is an external runtime dependency with erratic GFW behavior. Instead /map renders a protomaps PMTiles extract of the corridor (bbox 55,17→127,49, maxzoom 8, overzoomed to 12 by Leaflet) through protomaps-leaflet — REAL OSM cartography, served from our origin via a Range-supporting route (Next public/ doesn't guarantee Range, and pmtiles readers live on range requests). The extract lives in .data/basemap (gitignored, ro-mounted into the app container) and is fetched once per server by ops/fetch-basemap.sh; with the file absent the page silently uses the schematic SVG, so CI and fresh installs never break. The build host's egress policy blocks build.protomaps.com, which is exactly why the fetch happens on the owner's server, not at build time.
115. **Auto client codes continue the biggest GROUP, not the biggest number** (owner: sequence at 425 with one-off codes 777/5564/5909 produced 5910): `nextClientNumber` splits the existing suffixes into groups separated by gaps > 50, picks the group with the most members (ties → the higher one, so scattered codes still behave like the old MAX+1), and returns the first free number above it. A first attempt used a density heuristic (rank ≥ 60% of value); an adversarial review killed it — it silently degraded to MAX+1 for sequences starting at the fresh-install 100 until ~248 clients existed, latched onto outliers within ~1.67× the count, and let a tiny low run anchor the head below a much larger high cluster. The group rule has none of those regions and is explainable to the owner in one sentence. Same review surfaced three real hardening items now fixed: `substring(code from $n)` resolves to the REGEX overload when the offset is a bound parameter (used `substr` instead), the prefix must be regex-escaped and uppercased before it reaches the pattern (`clients_code_upper_check`), and unsafe-integer suffixes are filtered so the "first free number" loop cannot spin while holding the advisory lock.
116. **Client-code search is literal when the query has digits** (owner: receiving an unknown GS500 offered GS300 and hid the unclaimed path): `similarity(client_code, q) > 0.3` treated codes as fuzzy text, but a digit IS the identity of a code. `searchClients()` (now shared by receiving/issue/finance) matches codes with ILIKE only whenever the query contains a digit, keeps trigram fuzziness for names and for digit-free code typos, and orders exact → prefix → similarity. The unclaimed escape hatch became the LAST row of the suggestion list (the list overlays the standalone button, so a hit used to bury it) and is labelled without the typed code so it can never be mistaken for a match; the standalone code-labelled button renders only once the search has ANSWERED — while a lookup is in flight the UI must not claim the code is unknown, since that button previously flashed under the operator's thumb for an existing code and filed the receipt as unclaimed.
117. **Demo data is bootstrap-only; the seed still runs every deploy** (owner: "demo accountlarni ham qayta qo'shib qo'ymasin"): the compose `migrate` service re-runs the seed on every update because that is how new permission codes reach the role matrix — but the same run used to re-insert missing demo users (password `demo1234`), demo clients/warehouses, example FX rates and the canonical receipt, resurrecting anything the owner deleted. Demo blocks are now gated on an EMPTY users table (`SEED_DEMO=1` overrides for test envs) while reference data stays unconditional. For servers that already carry them, `pnpm demo-users [--disable]` deactivates only the accounts that still verify against the demo password — a changed password means the account was adopted and is left alone — and never removes the last active super admin. Deactivation (not deletion) is the retirement mechanism: `users.active` is checked both at login and on every session lookup, and it keeps audit/receipt foreign keys intact.
118. **Driver tracking is trip-scoped and pairing happens in the warehouse worker's hands** (owner's flow: the loader installs the app on the driver's phone and grants permissions himself, which removes the usual driver-discipline failure mode): a `driver_devices` row belongs to a BATCH, its 6-character pair code is single-use (burned on exchange, ambiguous characters excluded), and the issued token is stored hashed exactly like a session token. When the batch closes, ingest answers 410 so the app stops and clears itself — no consent questions about tracking a person outside a trip. Ingest takes an ARRAY of fixes because the China→UZ corridor has long dead zones: the phone queues offline and re-flushes, so a partial unique index on (device_id, recorded_at) makes re-sends idempotent. The map prefers a fix newer than 90 minutes over the schedule estimate and labels which one it is showing; older fixes stay visible as "last real signal" while the estimator drives the marker again. iPhone/HarmonyOS are deliberately NOT app targets (App Store distribution and HarmonyOS NEXT signing both need business accounts the owner does not have) — the logist's manual pins feed the same `driver_positions` table with source='manual'.
119. **The driver app avoids Google Play services, third-party libraries and the Play Store alike** (owner: drivers in China use Android/iPhone/HarmonyOS, and our warehouse worker installs the app by hand): location comes from the framework `LocationManager` because Huawei and most mainland phones have no GMS, so the fused provider would simply never fire; networking is `HttpURLConnection` and storage a plain `SQLiteOpenHelper` queue, keeping the dependency list to core-ktx + appcompat so the CI build cannot break on resolution. Distribution is a direct APK from GitHub Actions artifacts — Play would demand a background-location review and does not exist in China anyway; the release build is debug-signed on purpose since nothing is published. iPhone and HarmonyOS NEXT are deliberately out of scope (App Store and Huawei signing both require business accounts the owner lacks): those trips stay on the logist's manual pins, which the map already merges with device fixes.
120. **The driver app trades live tracking for battery, and the battery exemption becomes a setup step** (owner's field round: "har 2-3 soatda bersa ham boladi", "batarey optimization"): continuous `requestLocationUpdates` was replaced by an `AlarmManager` (`setAndAllowWhileIdle`) → one fix → upload → sleep cycle, default 2 h (1/3 h selectable on the phone), with a 90 s acquisition window, early exit at ≤100 m accuracy and a 10-minute retry when a cycle comes back empty; a partial wake lock covers the cycle because an alarm only guarantees a few seconds of CPU. Inexact alarms are deliberate — `setExactAndAllowWhileIdle` needs a restricted permission and ±10 minutes is meaningless for a truck — which makes the Doze allowlist the load-bearing part, so `REQUEST_IGNORE_BATTERY_OPTIMIZATIONS` is now a mandatory step in the first-run chain (location → notifications → background → battery dialog → vendor auto-start), each step offered exactly once per run so a refusal cannot loop, then kept on screen as a red checklist row. The vendor auto-start list has no API to read or request, so it is opened by intent and confirmed by hand; `<queries>` declares the OEM security-centre packages, without which Android 11+ package visibility would hide those screens. The foreground notification became a new minimum-importance channel (importance can only be raised on an existing channel) and says nothing unless a permission is missing or a real backlog exists — a foreground service cannot drop its notification, but it can stop narrating. Consequence on the server: `FRESH_MINUTES` 90 → 480, since at a 2-3 h cadence a 90-minute window would mark almost every real fix stale and silently return the map to the estimate.
121. **Batch membership is what DEPARTED, not what `current_batch_id` still points at** (owner: manual acceptance never moved the counter and finishing declared the truck missing): unload clears `current_batch_id` on acceptance, so every screen that asked "which boxes are on this batch" lost them one by one as work progressed. `batchMemberFilter()` unions the live pointer with the `batch_departed` box_movements rows — written once at departure, never rewritten — and the unload snapshot uses it. The client-side companion: "accepted" means `status !== 'in_transit'`, never `status === 'in_stock'`, because a customs/distribution destination lands cargo in `ready_for_pickup` (spec 6.6) and matching on `in_stock` pinned the counter at zero for every real Uzbekistan arrival. Same rule applied to `resolveMissing('found_here')`, which hardcoded `in_stock` and left recovered boxes invisible to the pickup/issue flows. Consequence for the UI: `finishUnload` is destructive (it flags the remainder `missing_in_transit`), so it must never be the only one-tap action on the screen — `unloadRemaining()` accepts the outstanding manifest through the normal ingest path (idempotent via uuid5 over the batch id, so a double press replays) and the finish button now confirms with the count it is about to declare lost.
122. **A batch code is editable until the truck leaves, and never after** (owner: "YW-001/002 comes from the warehouse — let me type it myself sometimes"): `renameBatch` accepts a manual code while the batch is `forming`/`loading`, trims it, collapses inner whitespace and uppercases it (the unique index is case-sensitive, so a near-duplicate could otherwise hide behind case), rejects collisions with a case-insensitive lookup, and audits before/after. The cutoff is the point of no return: after departure the code is already on the invoice, the manifest, the packing list and whatever the customs agent received, so a rename would desynchronise the papers from the system — the owner accepted this when asked. Guarded by `plans.manage` rather than `batches.vehicle_info`: the code is the batch's identity on every document, not a logistics detail.
123. **Rarely-used fields fold, always-used fields stay open** (owner's round: notes and expenses are written on a minority of receipts, photos and files on every one): a shared `Panel` (native `<details>`, zero client JS, works in a server component) now wraps the VED documents, the driver pairing code and the receiving note+cost pair, while the attachment row stays visible. A collapsed panel that holds live data still says so — the driver panel badges the pending pair code, the receiving one dots when a draft already carries a note or a cost — so folding never hides state. Same mechanism for the batch board's archive drawer, which is the fix for finished batches becoming unreachable: membership/board queries filter statuses in SQL now, and the archive counts boxes from `batch_departed` movements because a closed batch owns none.
124. **Exports follow the reader; customs paperwork does not** (owner asked for English "for everyone"): the ten report XLSX builders take a locale and render headers through `reports/labels.ts` in all four languages, because those files are downloaded to be read by the person who asked for them. The invoice, packing list, manifest, packing-photo list, agent file and handover act instead use `documents/labels.ts`, which is bilingual RU/EN and deliberately locale-BLIND — their readers are an Uzbek customs officer and a Chinese forwarding agent, so following the interface language would let a manager working in English hand the border a paper it cannot process. Consequence found the hard way: Excel rejects `\ / ? * [ ] :` in a worksheet name, so a bilingual "Сводка / Summary" used as a tab made every manifest download 500 — sheet names now come from separate slash-free constants and two tests hold that line (no illegal character in a sheet name; all five documents generate). Box/crate stickers stay single-language: their words live in a fixed 100×100 mm layout that cannot be proof-printed from here, and a longer string risks clipping on the physical label.
125. **Staff Telegram messages follow the recipient; the client drafts inside them do not** (part 3 of the English rollout): `renderTelegramText` takes a locale and `sendPendingTelegram` reads it from the recipient's `users.locale`, so the same event is rendered once per reader — the wording lives in `notifications/labels.ts` rather than next-intl because the sender is a background worker with no request context. The exception is deliberate: the ReadyForPickup message carries two ready-to-forward drafts addressed to the CLIENT (uz + ru), and those follow the client's language, not the manager's — translating them into the manager's language would hand a Russian-reading manager an English text to send to an Uzbek client. The client cabinet bot stays Uzbek-only for now (DECISIONS #110): giving it a language would mean storing one per client and a switch button in the chat, which no current client needs.

126. **Management accounting, not double-entry bookkeeping** (Phase 2.4, owner's answer 1 "A"): no chart of accounts, no debit/credit, no balance sheet — the official books stay with the accountant, these numbers run the business. Four new tables (`expense_categories`, `money_accounts`, `expenses`, `recurring_expenses`, `account_transfers`, migration 0020) carry the overhead side that `cost_entries` never covered. The owner's answers shaped the rest: expense kinds and cash boxes are DATA he maintains, not enums in the code (answers 2–4); salaries are booked per employee (5); revenue lands on the charge date, because the price is agreed once customs is done and that is when the service is really finished (6); profit is `finance.reports` + `finance.expenses`, so a sales manager with `finance.view` sees client balances and never the margin (7); opening balances are entered so a screen balance matches the cash box (8); there is no tax line at all — the accountant runs that separately (9); every USD figure is shown with a soʻm equivalent at TODAY's rate, clearly labelled as a reading convenience while the frozen USD figures are what the reports add up (10). FX follows #108 exactly: the rate is frozen at entry, so correcting a rate later cannot rewrite a month already reported, and mistakes are voided with a reason, never deleted. `expense_categories.cash = false` is the one flag with logic behind it: a non-cash expense (depreciation) belongs in the P&L but must stay out of the cash flow, and adding such a kind must not need a code change. Transfers between our own accounts are recorded separately from expenses with an amount per side (a CNY cash box can fund a USD account) — without that the cash-flow report would read moving our own money as money leaving the business.
127. **Fixed costs are posted by a button, and the monthly P&L is honest about straddling** (Phase 2.4): `generateRecurring(month)` walks the active templates and inserts what is missing, skipping any template that already produced a non-voided expense for that month — so pressing twice cannot double-charge — and the accountant reviews what landed. A background job would have been worse, not better: a silent monthly insert quietly falsifies the P&L of any month where the rent changed or someone left. Related and stated on the P&L page itself: revenue lands on its charge date and cargo cost on its cost date, so a batch whose costs fell in July and whose price was agreed in August splits across two columns. That is correct for a period P&L, and "Profit by batch" — where both sides belong to the batch whatever month they were entered — is the period-free answer to "did that trip earn money?". Receivables ageing settles payments against the OLDEST charge first (standard AR practice) so a regular payer never shows up in the 90+ bucket merely for being a long-standing client.
128. **Drizzle renders a column unqualified in a single-table select — never interpolate `${table.column}` inside a correlated subquery** (found by the Phase 2.4 tests, and it had already bitten `warehouseFill` once): `db.select({...}).from(batches)` emits `select "id", "code" …`, so a `${batches.id}` written inside `(SELECT … FROM client_transactions ct WHERE ct.batch_id = ${batches.id})` reaches Postgres as a bare `"id"` and binds to the SUBQUERY's table. Per-batch profit therefore returned revenue 0 and cost 0 for every batch (uuid = uuid, no error, just silently false), while the box-count subquery died on `uuid = bigint` because `box_movements.id` is a bigserial. The rule is to write `${batches}.id` — drizzle renders the TABLE reference, which is unambiguous in both forms. Queries with a join are unaffected (drizzle qualifies as soon as more than one table is in play), which is why the older report queries in `reports/queries.ts` were correct and this one was not. WHERE clauses are also unaffected — only the select list drops the qualification.
129. **CRM is narrow on purpose, and its home screen is the call list** (Phase 2.3): the client registry, the Telegram cabinet, the money ledger, the debt gate and profit-per-client already existed, so CRM adds only what a kargo business actually loses money on — someone who asked about prices and was never called back, a client who quietly stopped shipping, and a conversation nobody wrote down. `/crm` therefore opens on "who am I calling today" (leads and clients in ONE list, oldest first, overdue flagged) rather than a dashboard; charts would bury the only thing that has to happen that day. `/crm/leads` is the funnel board with columns scrolling INSIDE the board — a 360 px phone cannot show eight stages, and this is the shape salespeople already know. Still no tariffs (DECISIONS #108): a lead carries a note about the price that was quoted, not a price list.
130. **Everything configurable is DATA, and `kind` is the only thing the code knows** (owner: "maximalna custom qilish imkoni bo'lsin, amoCRM/Bitrix kabi"): funnel stages, lead sources and the card fields all live in tables the owner edits — stages can be added, renamed, recoloured, reordered and deleted (a delete hands its leads to a stage the operator picks), and the code only ever reasons about `lead_stages.kind` ∈ open/won/lost. Colours come from a fixed seven-value palette because Tailwind compiles the classes it can see, so `bg-${color}-100` built at runtime would not exist in the stylesheet. Custom fields have nine types, and the type drives both the widget and the validation, so a date field can never hold "keyinroq"; answers live in `crm_field_values` keyed by field id rather than a jsonb blob, so a rename cannot orphan them, a partial form touches only the fields it renders, and clearing an answer deletes the row instead of storing an empty string (a checkbox is the exception — unticked is a real "no"). A field's TYPE is locked after creation: changing it would leave every stored answer in the old shape.
131. **One person, several codes — a layer above clients, never a merge** (owner: "ha birlashtiraylik"; closes DECISIONS #40, parked since M1): `crm_people` groups client cards for the sales side while each code keeps its own letters, stock, cargo history and cabinet link. Merging the rows would rewrite years of receipts and invalidate every printed label, which is why the person is an extra pointer rather than a rewrite. With hundreds of cards nobody groups by hand, so the screen suggests codes that share a phone using the cabinet's last-9-digit rule (DECISIONS #111) and the owner confirms. The follow-up date lives on the client card itself (`clients.next_action_at`) rather than in a CRM-only table, so one query answers "who am I calling today" for leads and clients together.
132. **CRM digests are per recipient and silent when empty**: today's follow-ups at 08:30 Tashkent (half an hour before the warehouse digest, so the two do not arrive as one wall of text) and the gone-quiet list on Monday 09:00. Each recipient gets THEIR OWN list — `crm.leads.view_all` decides whether that means everyone's — because a message about someone else's clients is a message people learn to swipe away. Both join the existing `digest` mute group: someone who muted the warehouse digest meant these too. The dormancy threshold is a setting (`crm_dormant_days`, default 60), and a client who never shipped is never listed — they are a lead with a card, and including them would bury the real signal.
133. **Kanban drag is hand-written on Pointer Events, and touch starts on a long press** (owner: "ushlab statusdan statusga o'tkazish imkoniyati yo'qmi"): HTML5 drag-and-drop does not fire on touch at all, and this board is a phone-first screen, so the gesture is built on pointer events — one code path for finger and mouse. Touch requires a ~250 ms hold before the card lifts, which is what keeps a normal swipe scrolling the board and a normal tap opening the card; a mouse skips the wait and starts once the pointer has actually moved 8 px. No drag-and-drop library: the package would be larger than the code it replaced and would still need this touch handling. Three things had to be right and each was found by testing rather than reasoning: an `<a>` is natively draggable and its native drag fires `pointercancel`, killing the gesture (`draggable={false}`); the board must take `touch-action: none` while a card is in the air or the same finger also pans it; and the drop target is resolved with `elementFromPoint`, which returns null outside the viewport — on a 360 px screen only ~76 px of the next column is on screen, so edge auto-scroll is not a nicety but the only way to reach a distant stage. The move is optimistic and reverts if the server refuses.
134. **Integration tests get a 20 s timeout, not the 5 s default**: most of this suite drives a real Postgres — a single case can walk a receipt, a plan, two scans, a departure and a cost recompute, landing at 3–5 s. Under ordinary load that made healthy tests fail on time, twice (m3-planning, then m6-costing), and a timeout that fires on a working test teaches people to re-run instead of to look.
135. **The interface is a design system, not per-screen styling** (owner: "hamma narsa har qayoqda yotgandek tuyiladi"): colours, radii, shadows and type live as CSS variables mapped into Tailwind (`brand`, `ink`, `surface`, `line`), and the shared classes every screen already used — `.card`, `.btn-*`, `.input`, and now `.table`, `.chip`, `.section-title` — carry the redesign, so 67 screens changed appearance without 67 edits. A `PageHeader` / `Section` / `EmptyState` / `Stat` set gives every page the same shape; the diagnosis was that the app had no shared page structure at all, which is what "scattered" actually meant.
136. **One navigation model feeds the home tiles, the sidebar and the phone's tab bar** (`rbac/nav.ts`): a screen is declared once with its icon, its permissions and a tab-bar priority, so it can never appear in one place and be missing from another. On a phone the four highest-priority destinations the actor may see sit in a fixed bottom bar with everything else behind "•••"; from `md` up the same list is a sidebar. Before this, moving between areas meant returning to the home tiles every time.
137. **Icons are hand-drawn SVG, not emoji and not a package**: emoji rendered differently on every Chinese Android phone in the warehouse — different shapes, different weights, sometimes a box — and they were the app's loudest amateur signal. The 35-glyph set in `components/ui/icon.tsx` is smaller than an icon dependency, ships no runtime, and follows one rule (24×24, 1.75 stroke, currentColor) so an icon never shouts over the text beside it.
138. **A working screen owns the whole phone** (found by the e2e suite, not by looking): receiving, plan building, crate building, issuing and the scan modes each carry their own fixed action bar, and the new tab bar sat on top of it and swallowed every tap — seven specs failed at once. Those routes are modes rather than destinations and each has its own way out, so the tab bar hides on them instead of fighting for the z-index.
139. **The brand is the logo's own red, sampled from the file** (owner sent the GSR GROUP lockup): `#B80000` is the mark's dominant colour, and the whole palette is built from it, so the interface and the logo cannot drift apart. The mark ships as a white-keyed PNG (the source was red-on-white with no alpha) so it sits on both themes; the same file makes the PWA icons, which is what appears when someone adds the app to a phone's home screen. A plain `<img>` rather than `next/image`: a 32 px logo gains nothing from the optimiser and would cost an extra request through it.
140. **Dark mode is a cookie and one CSS block** (owner: "ha kerak qorong'i rejim"): every colour already resolved through CSS variables, so the theme is a single `:root[data-theme='dark']` override rather than a `dark:` class on a thousand elements — including the brand tints, which invert (the `bg-brand-50 text-brand-800` icon chip has to become a dark tint with light text) and the semantic colours, which brighten (`green-700` on a dark card is unreadable). The choice lives in a cookie, not localStorage, so the server renders `data-theme` into the first HTML and a dark phone never flashes white; with no cookie the CSS follows the system preference, so most people never choose. Getting there needed a 112-file sweep replacing hardcoded `bg-white` / `text-gray-*` / `bg-blue-*` with tokens — and it caught an e2e assertion written against `border-amber-300`, a styling class that was never a contract; that photo now carries a test id instead.
141. **The tab bar is per role, not per permission** (owner: "skladchilar prixod/yuklash/qabul, sotuvchilar CRM, buxgalterlar pul bilan bog'liq joyni ishlatadi"): one global priority order cannot serve three jobs — the list that puts receiving first for a warehouse operator puts it first for the accountant, who never opens it. `PRIMARY_BY_ROLE` names the four screens each role actually lives in, anything they may not see is skipped so the bar still fills, and a unit test asserts the three cases the owner described. The tab bar also uses SHORT labels (`navShort`): "Управленческий учёт" under a 60 px icon pushed the ••• button off a 360 px screen, while the sidebar and home tiles keep the full name.
142. **A rate is entered the way a person says it: "1 USD = 12 345 UZS"** (owner's round): the FX form asked for `rate_to_usd` — the reciprocal, 0.000081 — which is a number nobody quotes and everybody mistypes. The screen now takes units-per-dollar and `toRateToUsd` inverts it; `perUsd` inverts it back for the list, rounding to a sensible number of places by magnitude (whole so'm, cents for a mid-sized rate, four places for a CNY-style figure). Storage stays `rate_to_usd` because every frozen figure in `cost_entries`, `client_transactions` and `expenses` is that direction and a storage flip would rewrite settled money. The precision had to move with it: `numeric(18,8)` holds 1/12345 as 0.00008100, which reads back as 12 346 so'm per dollar — an off-by-one in a number the owner typed himself, and real money on a large invoice. Migration 0023 widens all four columns to `numeric(24,12)`. Note the column on `cost_entries` is named `fx_rate_used`, not `rate_to_usd`.
143. **The driver's pairing code is minted with the batch, in the same transaction** (owner: "code shu plan yaratilgani o'zida yaratilib bu sarlavhaga yozib qo'yilsa bo'ladimi"): both batch-creation paths — plan approval and quick batch — call `mintBatchPairCode`, so there is no window in which a trip exists without a code, and the batch header carries it in large type. The loader reads it off the header while the driver is standing at the gate instead of scrolling to a panel and pressing a button. Pairing still burns the code (DECISIONS #118), so the header shows it only while it is unclaimed and the manual "new code" button stays for the second phone or a lost pairing.
144. **The plan editor shows the same figures as the stock table, and switches them to what you are TAKING** (owner: "plan berayotganda kg/m³ ko'rinib tursin, sklad ostatkaga o'xshab"): kg per box, Σ kg, m³ and the colour-coded density now sit on every row, because a plan is built by weight and volume and those numbers were only available in the totals bar at the bottom. Once boxes are ticked the row's Σ figures follow the SELECTION rather than the shelf — on a partial take, the shelf figure answers a question nobody asked.
145. **"Where is my cargo and what do I owe" is one read model, rendered three ways** (`finance/client-cargo.ts`): the owner asked the same question from the client card, the finance ledger and a sales manager's own book, and three separate queries would have drifted apart the first time one of them was fixed. `clientCargo()` returns where the boxes are (grouped by warehouse and by stock/transit/ready, with kg and m³), every trip they travelled on, and the money on each trip; the `CargoSummary` component renders it with a `money` flag so a viewer without finance rights sees the cargo half and not the balances. Trips come from `batch_departed` movements rather than `current_batch_id` (DECISIONS #121) — accepting a box clears the pointer, so a live-pointer query would erase a client's whole history at the moment the cargo arrived.
146. **Debt is attributed to a trip by settling payments oldest-first, not by tagging payments** (owner: "qarz qaysi yukdan kelgan"): a payment carries no batch — a client pays a round number against whatever they owe — so the ledger cannot know which cargo it clears without asking. FIFO settlement is the standard answer and, more importantly, it is the rule the receivables ageing report already uses, so the two screens can never give a client different answers about which invoice is still open. A charge with no batch (a manual correction) is reported separately rather than spread across trips.
147. **Landed cost per client comes from the allocation table, not a re-computation** (owner: "rastamojka summalarini qayerda klientga kiritamiz — narxni shakllantirish uchun"): customs is entered ONCE against the batch and the existing engine already splits every entry down to the box, so `batchLandedCostByClient` is a sum over `cost_allocations` grouped by the box's client. Two figures, because they answer different questions: the total is everything spent on that cargo end to end (China-side receipt costs included) and is what a price has to beat; the batch share is what this trip added. Margin is shown only once a price exists — before that every row would read as a 100 % loss, which is noise, not information.
148. **A client card is not an admin screen** (found by the new e2e): the card lives under `/admin/clients/[id]` for historical reasons, and the admin section's cosmetic gate meant a sales manager clicking a client in their own call list, the dormant list or "my clients" was bounced to the home screen — three CRM features that had never worked for the role they were built for. The gate now also admits `clients.view_own` / `crm.leads`, the card checks its own permission, and the edit form, the deactivate button and the Telegram-cabinet panel are `clients.manage` only. The sub-nav is not rendered at all when a viewer has no admin tabs — an empty strip above every card is worse than none.
149. **The funnel has two shapes, and the phone's is not a kanban** (owner: "mobileda kanban view'ni ishlashga qulay qilib ber"): eight columns side by side on a 360 px screen leaves ~76 px of the next stage visible, so dragging a card to a stage you cannot see is a multi-second edge-scroll that misses as often as it lands — the gesture DECISIONS #133 built works, but the layout it works on does not. On a phone the board is now one stage at a time: a scrollable strip of stage tabs with counts (the funnel at a glance), that stage's cards full width, and a one-tap "→ next stage" button on each card with a bottom sheet behind "⋯" for anything else. The drag board stays from `md` up, where every column fits and dragging is genuinely the fastest move. Both are rendered and toggled with CSS rather than a measured breakpoint: reading the window in JavaScript renders the wrong shape for the first frame, and this is the CRM's home screen. The move logic is shared, so a lost deal asks for its reason whichever shape asked for the move. Consequence for the tests: Playwright gained a `desktop-chromium` project matching `*.desktop.spec.ts`, because a drag test in a 360 px viewport now exercises markup that is `display: none`.
150. **Handover is a warehouse setting, not a permission** (owner: "har sklad uchun «mijozga berish» sozlamasi — faqat TAS/AND'da"): `scan.issue` said who MAY hand cargo over, and every warehouse operator holds it, so the Chinese warehouses got a handover screen no client will ever stand in front of. `warehouses.issues_to_clients` says WHERE it happens; the screen lists only those warehouses and says so plainly when the viewer has none. Migration 0024 seeds the flag from the warehouse type rather than defaulting it to false — customs/distribution warehouses ARE the collection points, and a migration that silently switched handover off everywhere would be a production outage on the morning it shipped.
151. **An expected arrival is a promise, not stock** (owner's item 3): the sales side hears "five boxes to Yiwu on Friday" days before those boxes exist anywhere in this system, and until now that sentence lived in a chat while the warehouse found out when a courier walked in. `expected_arrivals` holds no boxes, no letters and no money — it is a row a warehouse reads in the morning. It closes ITSELF when the real receipt is confirmed, inside the receipt's own transaction, but only on an unambiguous match (exactly one waiting row for that client at that warehouse): with two open promises there is no way to know which receipt answers which, and guessing would close one that is still genuinely outstanding. The close can never fail a receipt — a planning aid that can block goods coming in is worse than no planning aid.
152. **`boxes.id IN (SELECT …)` as a JOIN condition is a table scan per row** (found by the new arrivals test, which took 28.7 s): the incoming-trucks query joined boxes to batches through a correlated subquery over `box_movements`, so Postgres walked the whole box table once per batch — a cost that grows with every receipt the company has ever taken. Joining `box_movements` directly and then `boxes` on its `box_id` uses the indexes and returns the same rows in 0.36 s. The general rule for this codebase: batch membership is a JOIN through `box_movements`, never a subquery in a join predicate.
153. **"Trucks" is a fleet view, not a settings screen** (owner: "/trucks o'rniga «qaysi partiya qayerga yetib bordi»"): the truck PRESETS — names and capacities — are opened twice a year and now live at `/admin/trucks` with the rest of the settings. `/trucks` answers what a logist actually opens a page called trucks for: which batch is loading, which is on the road, which has landed and where each one physically is — the arrival if it has one, otherwise the newest GPS fix from the driver's phone, otherwise the logist's manual pin, and it says which of the three it is showing.
154. **A test that walks away from a debt breaks the next run** (found on the second full e2e pass): m9 priced a client and left the charge unpaid, which armed the handover debt gate and made m5-issue fail — on a suite that shares one database in file order, leftover state IS the next test's input. m9 now settles its own charge before finishing. Related: file names carry the ordering, and lexicographic sorting puts `m10-…` between `m1-…` and `m2-…`, so a spec that needs a departed truck cannot be called m10; the arrivals spec is `m9b-arrivals.spec.ts`.
