The sweep that flags lateness claims its rows in the UPDATE before anything leaves (0082's rule, three rounds old) and reaches the `ved.docs` holders — not just the requester and the owner — when nobody has taken it, because an untaken late request is a team failure and the round-28 routing sent its only alert away from the team. Deliberately NOT built: the list engine on the queue (ten live rows do not need saved views), and any money on it at all — not a balance, not a quote, not a margin (round 91's fence, and `ved_manager` happens to hold `finance.manage`, so testing as that role would have made a leak look safe).

749. **The blocker three of the five reviewers found independently: the VED person could not open the materials of the requests the bot creates.** `attachments/access.ts`'s `crm_activity` branch admits `ved.docs` for a note on a DEAL (#299-301) and not for one on a LEAD — and `ROLE_MATRIX.ved_manager` holds neither `crm.leads` nor `clients.manage`, while the bot's stranger path lands on a lead BY CONSTRUCTION. Every photo and every invoice on the whole bot half of the queue would have 404'd on the day it shipped, against the owner's own law 11 («everything the seller submitted is shown to the VED as-is»). The widening is exactly as narrow as the promise: a lead note opens for `ved.docs` **only when a calculation request points at it** (`calc_requests.note_id`, indexed for the per-file lookup) — the seller deliberately handed those materials over; their other lead correspondence is still none of the customs manager's business, which the second test pins. Two more fences came from the same review and are in the service: the note is MINTED by `openCalcRequest` under the id the browser pre-bound its uploads to and an id that already exists is REFUSED (`note_taken`) rather than adopted — otherwise a posted uuid would attach a stranger's note, and its files, to a request that widens the gate over them — and whatever the source, the note must sit on the same card as the request (`note_foreign`). Stated and not fixed: `ved.docs` is now a bundle of four unrelated powers (deal write, company-wide Telegram supervision since round 33, customs papers, and this queue), so granting a new person the calc queue grants them the rest; splitting it needs a new permission code, which #170 says the seed cannot reach on a customised role — the owner's call, made with the cost written down.

750. **Deploy morning has TWO error codes, and the one this round needed was the missing one.** The repo's only stale-schema guard tested `42P01` (undefined_table), which a release that adds a brand-new TABLE produces. Phase A adds COLUMNS to a table that has existed since 0052, and that fails with **`42703`, undefined_column** — so the guard would have passed it straight through to the error page. `isServerBehind` now accepts both, and it matters more here than usual because the new panel renders inside two screens that already work: on the morning the app is a release ahead of the migration, an uncaught read in `CalcPanel` would have taken the DEAL CARD down with it, and `vedFlowCounts` would have taken the VED manager's home screen. Both fail soft. Also from the migration review, and both applied: `IF EXISTS`/`IF NOT EXISTS` on every statement (a dump restored onto a behind ledger re-runs the file, and drizzle wraps all pending migrations in ONE transaction, so a `42701` there rolls back everything behind it too), and `section`/`source` are NULLABLE rather than `NOT NULL DEFAULT` — a default would have stamped every pre-round-46 row as a «podklyuch» job that nobody ever asked for, which is the opposite of 0084's own rule that NULL reads «nobody has said». The sweep's first run after the deploy would have telegraphed the owner about year-old requests, so the migration stamps `overdue_notified_at` on every row still open from before it.

751. **Two test-hygiene defects this round created and the run itself found.** (a) The bot's landing now opens a request, so `calc-intake.integration.test.ts` — a file this round otherwise never touched — started leaving rows in a COMPANY-WIDE queue: a number on the VED home, rows in front of the next spec's own, and after two hours a Telegram to the owner about a fixture. It sweeps them by WINDOW («this actor, since this file started») rather than by id, because `landIntake` mints deals of its own and there is no list of entities to walk. (b) `m9zo` captured its request from the QUEUE's first row — which belongs to whoever asked first, i.e. an earlier spec — so it took and finished somebody else's request while passing perfectly. The panel now links each open request for a `ved.docs` holder, and the spec captures ITS OWN id from that link. The same run also produced the round's most ordinary bug: `db.execute` hands timestamps back as STRINGS (the typed query builder returns Dates), so `row.due_at.getTime()` threw the first time anything asked whether a request was late — the queue screen would have been a 500 on its own first visit, and no type in the file said otherwise.

752. **Round 110 — «xitoyda juda sekin, planlarni yuklayotgan paytda ishlatib bo'lmayabti», and the answer was bytes nobody was counting.** The measurement first, because the shape of it decides everything: `GET /api/batches/<id>/planned` — the manifest both scan screens live on — came back **28,506 bytes with `Accept-Encoding: gzip` asked for and no `Content-Encoding` at all**, while the same body gzipped is **975** (29×). The same server DOES compress the HTML it renders (124 KB → 36 KB on /login), which is why nobody had noticed: **Next compresses what it renders and not a Route Handler's own `Response`**, and Caddy in front of it compresses nothing unless told to — the generated Caddyfile has two directives and `encode` was not one of them. So every API answer this company sends left the machine raw, over the slowest link it has. At 268 bytes a box row that is 52 KB for a 200-box truck, 157 KB for 600, 262 KB for 1000 — re-read every 15 seconds, per phone.

753. **The unload screen re-read the whole truck after EVERY scan.** Round 9 (#248-250) fixed exactly this on the LOADING screen and split its flush into «send the queue» and «also re-read the snapshot»; the unload screen — the one Kashgar uses, on the same China→Europe link — was written with `accept()` ending in an unconditional `void flush()`, and its `flush` always did the snapshot GET. Unloading a 200-box truck therefore pulled the manifest **200 times**: at 157 KB that is ~31 MB of pointless traffic for one truck, on top of the 200 POSTs that carry the actual work, with three scans a second all in flight at once. MEASURED in a browser before and after: four accepts cost four snapshot GETs before, **zero** after. It now carries the loading screen's two mechanisms verbatim — `flush({ sync })` and the `flushSoon()` coalescing pair — and the follow-up recurses through a local `run()` rather than the callback's own name, because the compiler's lint refuses a const that reads itself (the loading screen's identical shape lints clean; that difference is unexplained and not worth a round).

754. **`platform/http/json.ts`: gzip and an ETag, in the app rather than only at the edge.** Two jobs, both about bytes that need not travel. The tag is the **hash of the body we were about to send**, so «unchanged» is exact by construction — it cannot lie about a payload that moved, whatever the queries did — and `If-None-Match` is parsed as the LIST it is, with the `-gzip` suffix some proxies append stripped, `*` honoured. gzip runs on the threadpool (`promisify(zlib.gzip)`), never inline: this process is the whole app's ceiling (round 74) and 250 KB of JSON is CPU it should not spend on the event loop. Bodies under 1 KB are sent as they are. **The client had to ask explicitly**: the screens send `If-None-Match` from a ref, because measurement showed the service worker's NetworkFirst handler re-fetching the URL itself and no conditional request ever reaching the server through it. Result on a real phone-shaped browser with the worker installed: mount 200 gzip **990 bytes**, then **304 with no body** on every quiet 15-second tick — and a colleague's change still arrives on the very next tick (proven by inserting a crate mid-probe and watching the 304 become a 200).

755. **Caddy compresses everything else — and this time it was TESTED here, not on deploy morning.** The compose file generates its Caddyfile inline, so the usual objection applies (#472: a change nobody can try before the deploy is a trap). Answered by downloading a real Caddy 2.8 and putting it in front of this build in exactly the production shape: without `encode`, an API body went out at 5,793 bytes; with `encode zstd gzip`, **1,283** (zstd); a response the app had ALREADY gzipped passed through untouched at 990 (no double encoding); and a `Range` read of the map's `.pmtiles` stayed a plain 206 with the exact bytes asked for. Both generated Caddyfiles (`https` and `https-local`) were run through `caddy validate` as `printf` writes them. That is what makes the one-line edit safe: it covers every route the app-level helper does not, including the ones nobody has thought about yet.

756. **The service worker was reloading the page on every reconnect, mid-scan.** `@serwist/next` defaults `reloadOnOnline: true`, which is `window.addEventListener('online', () => location.reload())` — confirmed in the built bundle, not just in the option's type. Warehouse wifi in Yiwu and Kashgar flaps all day, so the scan screens were reloading themselves in the middle of a pallet: the camera restarts, the operator loses their place, and the phone re-downloads the shell and the truck over the worst link the company has. Nothing here needs it — those screens carry their own offline banner, their own cached snapshot and an IndexedDB outbox, and every other live screen re-reads on its own beat. Off now, and the built bundle re-checked for the listener afterwards. **This is why «ishlatib bo'lmayabti» is the right word and «sekin» is not**: a screen that keeps restarting is not slow, it is unusable.

757. **Two smaller ones on the same path, both measured.** (a) Both scan screens downloaded the snapshot **twice on mount** — the mount effect read it, and the flush effect's immediate `flush({sync:true})` read it again; the first call takes no `sync` now, and the browser probe shows one 200 where there were two. (b) The 15-second tick never asked whether anyone was looking: a phone locked in a pocket kept polling, which costs the phone nothing and the SERVER a real query, times every phone in the warehouse (round 74's one-process ceiling). It now ticks only while `document.visibilityState === 'visible'` and syncs immediately on the way back, so a woken screen still opens on current data. Also: the crate block of the snapshot ships ACTIVE crates only — a dissolved crate has had its members' `crate_id` cleared since round 31, so it can never expand a scan to anything, and it rode that payload for ever (one warehouse: 612 crates, 1,632 box codes, 56 KB on a five-box batch) — and the `IN ${array}` raw fragment became `inArray` (the recorded footgun; empirically this drizzle renders it correctly, but the empty array is the real hazard and `inArray` states it).

758. **What this round did NOT do, said plainly.** The 304 saves the BYTES and not the queries: a quiet tick still costs the server the same ~67 ms of database work, because the tag is computed from the finished body. The honest next step is a cheap version probe (the round-108 chat pulse's shape) so an unchanged truck costs one indexed count instead of the full read — worth doing when the phone count grows, not before, and it is written down here so the next round does not re-derive it. Also left, with reasons: the `available` branch still ships up to 1,500 stock rows inside every quick-batch snapshot (compression takes it from ~400 KB to a few tens of KB, and the list is what makes the pick sheet work offline — but its cap truncates SILENTLY, which is a correctness bug of its own); `batchMemberFilter`'s `OR` runs twice per snapshot; and HTTP/3 is advertised to every phone while compose publishes TCP 443 only, so every client tries QUIC, fails, and falls back — harmless per request, and worth a line in the compose file the next time that file is opened.

759. **Round 111, item 1 — the camera icon opened the camera and nothing else, and removing that unmasked a defect it had been hiding for years.** His «skladchilar prixod qilganda kamera iconni bossa rasimga olish kamerasi ochilyabti lekin shuni fildan tanlash yokida rasimga olish qilib ochadigan yoli borku, file yuklaydigan tugmaga o'xshasa». The cause is one attribute: `capture="environment"` on four `<input type="file">` elements tells the phone to skip its own chooser and open the camera directly, so the photograph of the pallet taken five minutes ago — or the invoice already on the phone — could not be used at all, and the operator had to retake it standing in the aisle. Gone from all four (the per-lot tile, the receipt's general photos, the rasxod chek and the handover evidence), which is a one-tap trade he asked for: on every device the camera is now one press further away and the gallery and the file browser exist. **`accept="image/*"` deliberately STAYS on the photo slots, and the rule that separates the two is mechanical**: those ids are pushed into `lot.photoIds`/`generalPhotoIds`, which `lotsValid()` counts as the confirm gate and `LightboxImg` renders as an `<img>` — a PDF there would OPEN the confirm button and then draw broken on the receipt, the stock table and the label sheet. `accept` may only be dropped from a control whose handler has an `isImage` branch, and today that is exactly one: `addReceiptFiles`, the 📎 beside it, which is the door for documents and has no `accept` at all.

760. **The re-pick silence: three of the four inputs never cleared `value`, and `capture` was what hid it.** A file input fires `change` only when the selection CHANGES; picking the same file twice fires nothing. With the camera forced, every shot was a fresh temp file, so the comparison never matched and nobody ever saw it. Open the gallery and the natural retry after a failed upload — pick the same photo again — becomes a tap that does nothing at all, on the control that gates the confirm button, in the warehouse where uploads fail. Round 97's exact symptom («the button stays grey and nothing says why») arriving through a new door. All four now clear it, and the ORDER is load-bearing: **the handler is called first and `value` cleared second**, because clearing it empties `input.files` — reversed, the upload silently sends nothing. Safe only because every handler snapshots the list synchronously before its first await. The 📎 sibling had carried the same bug with no `capture` to hide it and was fixed in the same commit rather than left as a known one; `expense-request-fold` already had it right and is the model the other three copied. No browser test in this suite can see either half — Playwright's `setInputFiles` dispatches `change` unconditionally, and `capture` is a hint headless Chromium does not draw — so `tests/unit/photo-inputs.test.ts` is source-shape and pins both rules including the order, red-proven three ways.

761. **A file the browser cannot read as a photograph stopped being «check the connection».** `browser-image-compression` refuses a non-image with a bare `Error`, indistinguishable from a network failure, and all three photo callers caught it as `photoUploadFailed` — «Rasm yuklanmadi — aloqani tekshiring», which sends a warehouse with perfect wifi to look at its router (#669's own mistake). `compressPhoto` now applies the test ITSELF and throws a typed `PhotoUnreadable`, which every caller tells apart; the same type covers the HEIC case, which passes `/^image/` and fails later at decode, because both end in the same honest sentence. One new key, `common.photoOnly`, in all four bundles — deliberately NOT the existing `fileTypeUnsupported`, whose own text lists «PDF, Excel, Word» as allowed and would be a lie on a slot that takes images only. The reviewer who proposed reusing that key had not read its words; a key's NAME is not its meaning.

762. **Round 111, item 2 — a seller may open a client code, and is its manager.** His «hamma account uchun navbarda + iconkada lid ochish bor, shu yerga klient code ochishni ham qoshishimiz kerak boladi». The «+» has offered a client since round 107; it was gated on `clients.manage`, which `sales_manager` does not hold — so the person who signs the customer could raise a lead but not the code that goes on their cartons, and had to ask an admin. `canMintClient` / `CLIENT_MINT_PERMISSIONS` now live in `platform/clients/service.ts` and BOTH doors ask the one predicate: the app bar decides what is drawn, the action decides what happens, and a hand-posted call meets the same rule. The action drops `authorize()` — it takes one code and this door answers to two — for the house pair-gate idiom (`getActor` + predicate) from `bitimlar/actions.ts`. **The three doors that were NOT widened keep their single-code gate** and a test counts them: the full admin form, the update and the active toggle, because widening those would additionally hand over the typed-code field, the manager picker and the internal notes — a separate decision, and the owner's. His answer settled the manager question and it was also the only workable one: «kod ochgan odamning ozi unga ozi manager bolib yozilishi kerak». Round 91 keys every seller's read on `clients.sales_manager_id`, so a client minted without it is invisible to whoever just minted it — and worse, `ReceiptConfirmed`, `ReadyForPickup` and `BoxIssued` all return an EMPTY recipient list for a client with no manager, so nobody is told anything about their cargo, ever. The quick door has no picker and stamps the actor; the full form keeps its picker and falls back to the actor. `createClient` itself is untouched, because `scripts/import-clients.ts` and every bulk path depend on it defaulting to null.

763. **«Batafsil →» would have bounced the very person the door was opened to.** `/admin/clients/new` is `clients.manage` at the page AND at its action, so a seller pressing it lands on the home screen with what they typed gone. The link is now offered per KIND, from a `fullForms` list the layout computes — the component still holds no permission knowledge, the way the nav receives a ready list rather than the rules behind it. Hiding rather than widening is the v1, stated: opening that page is the bigger decision above. Two smaller things measured in the same pass and both kept: the kind chooser became a CHIP STRIP above the boxes instead of a menu in front of them (round 60 opened straight into a text box because there was one kind; a menu would have charged the whole sales team an extra tap on their most frequent action to reach a door they use weekly), and with one kind the strip does not render at all, so nothing changes for anybody who has only one.

764. **The client door got the duplicate warning the lead door has had since round 79 — and it is a warning, never a refusal.** One person holding 444, 555 and 777 is this company's normal shape (#407), so a second code for the same phone is often right. But the seller cannot open the client book, so without this they mint a sibling code blind and the cargo, the calls and the chat then sit on the code nobody is looking at. `activeClientsByPhone` answers with the codes AND the names; the same press again mints anyway; any edit to the name or the number retires the warning, so the second press can never apply to something the person has since changed. Its own error code (`duplicateCode`), because the lead path's words are about a lead. **The panel renders its refusals through a TEMPLATE key**, which `i18n-keys.test.ts` cannot see by design — so `tests/unit/quick-create-errors.test.ts` compares the codes the two quick actions can actually return against all four bundles, scoped to those two function bodies. Its first version scanned whole FILES and demanded `quick.error` words for `winLead`'s refusals: a fence that fails on something correct is one the next person deletes.

765. **Round 111, item 3 — a new client code opens a deal, and the reason this needed a whole round is what the deal would have BROKEN.** His «klient kod ochilganda avtomatik bitimda shu odam bn bitim ochilsin yangi bolib». The deal itself is four lines. The danger is that `priceControlOnReceipt` reads «does this client have an open deal» to choose between two opposite instructions (round 107 item 3): with one, cargo that arrives unlinked says «📎 biriktiring» naming the codes; with none, «💰❓ narx qo'ying». Give every client an empty deal and the first arrival for nearly every new customer flips to «attach it» — pointing at a shell with no price — and the seller who follows that instruction reaches the deviation branch, where a deal with no quote is `incomparable`, `worthAlerting` is false, and **the alarm is gone for good**. The cargo reaches Tashkent with nobody having agreed a number, which is the single thing the whole of price control exists to prevent. Fixed on both sides off one idea — *an empty deal is not something to attach to*: `openDealCodes` counts only deals that carry a price or already carry goods lines, and a receipt LINKED to a quote-less deal now emits `UnquotedCargo` (naming the deal, so the seller lands where the price goes) instead of falling silent. The test is `deal.quotedAmount === null` and not `deviation.incomparable` — incomparable means «no quoted SIZE», and a deal priced at $4,000 with no volume on it is properly quoted and must not alarm. **The linked half was already live**: round 107 made `winLead` always open a deal, unpriced when the lead carried no quote, so every client won from an unquoted lead has been in this trap in production since. Red-proven both ways, and the second proof failing with the branch removed IS the demonstration that today's tree has the hole.

766. **Where a deal nobody asked for belongs on the board, and where the hook belongs in the code.** The top of a column is for a card a PERSON raised — they are about to work on it. A shell the system opened is not that, and the board draws forty cards per stage ordered by `board_order ASC`, so fifty client codes in a week would push real priced work off the slice. `bottomOfColumn` joins `topOfColumn` and `createDeal` takes an explicit `atBottom`, default false: ONLY the client-code door passes it, so `winLead`, the deal form and the bot's landing all keep the top and not one existing behaviour moved. The hook lives in the two APP doors and deliberately NOT in `createClient`: `scripts/import-clients.ts` mints the whole book through the service and would open ~1,700 shells, and `winLead` already opens its own deal after minting a client, so a service-level hook would give every won lead two. It runs AFTER the client is committed, in its own try/catch — the code is what goes on the cartons, and losing it because the funnel has no open stage would be a far worse trade than a deal raised by hand; the panel draws the deal link only when an id comes back, so «code minted, deal not» is a state the screen can tell the truth about. In the full form it must run BEFORE `redirect()`, which throws `NEXT_REDIRECT` — anything after it is unreachable, and a try/catch around it would swallow the redirect itself. The wizard's deal picker is filtered to priced deals at the ROUTE and not in `openDealsForClient`, whose other consumer is round 38's repair door, where attaching to an empty deal is exactly the point.


767. **The VED workspace, phase B: a price on the screen is a DRAFT, a price in `calc_versions` is a FACT, and the seal is the one door between them.** The design was scouted by five parallel readers and then judged by a six-lens adversarial workflow that raised 90 findings, confirmed 72 and called 26 of them blockers — every one absorbed before a line was written. The draft is recomputed from the dictionaries on every render, so a baza corrected this morning changes it; the version is written once, carries the tariff ROW and every rate that made it, and nothing ever updates it, which is what makes «what was this client told a year ago» answerable at all. Migration **0086** adds the three dictionaries the company has never had (`calc_bazas`, `calc_rates`, `calc_freight_tariffs` — all versioned by `effective_date` and read the way `fx_rates`/`rateFor` has always been read), the `calc_groups` a calculation is made of, `calc_extras` pointing at the EXISTING cost-type dictionary so phase E compares like with like, and `calc_versions`. The one deliberate departure from `rateFor` is written into the readers and not the schema: **there is no earliest-row fallback.** `rateFor` has one because a cost entered before the first exchange rate still has to convert into something; here a missing baza means nobody has ever priced this product, and the fallback would also let a row dated next month price today's calculation.

768. **The engine never returns a number it had to invent.** `4 * null` is `0` in JavaScript; the goods a seller forwards from Telegram routinely carry no weight and often no quantity; and `calc_rates` is born EMPTY on the day this deploys. An engine that multiplied happily would therefore have sealed a locked, client-facing «rastamojka: $0.00» on a real consignment — the single worst outcome this module can produce. So `pricing.ts` is pure and every entry point answers `{ok:true, …}` or `{ok:false, reason}`: `baza_missing` and `measure_missing` NAME the offending item, `rates_missing` refuses a group whose rate nobody decided (a lgota is a real zero and passes; an absent rate is not), `band_missing`/`band_ambiguous` refuse the freight, `discount_exceeds_total` refuses the total. The screen renders ⚠ and the reason, never `$0` — #533's «≈ $0 ⚠» is for a KNOWN zero and the two must not look alike — and every refusal is also a seal blocker, listed by name under the button. **The priced unit is the ITEM, not the group**, which was the review's first blocker: his law says a baza belongs to a PRODUCT and one TNVED code holds several products with different bazas, so `calc_request_items` carries `baza_usd`/`baza_basis`/`baza_source` and the customs value is a SUM over items. Two products under one code at $8/kg and $3/kg priced at either number is nearly half out. Rounding follows what a broker does by hand and in that order — value rounded first, duty from the rounded value, VAT from value + rounded duty — because every figure the screen prints must be reproducible from the figures printed beside it; a table whose lines do not add up to its total reads as a broken screen.

769. **His freight tariff is seeded VERBATIM, and the lookup refuses the two holes in it rather than closing them for him.** Read as bare lower bounds — the obvious implementation — his table answers every density, and his table does not: 900-999 kg/m³ is covered by no row, 700 is covered by two, and 1000 steps from $320/m³ to $0.55/kg. Each is money: 30 m³ at 950 kg/m³ is **$9,600 read down or $15,675 read up**, and the lower-bound reading picks the cheaper one silently, every time, for ever. So the band carries `max_density` as well as `min_density`, the twelve rows per zone are seeded exactly as he wrote them (only when the table is empty, at a FIXED `effective_date` — a row dated «whenever the seed ran» makes two installations disagree about what is in force), and `bandFor` answers `band_missing` for the gap and `band_ambiguous` for the overlap. His answers become dated rows he can see on the tariff screen, which is the only way a tariff decision leaves a record. **The band is looked up by a WHOLE kg/m³** (`bandDensityOf` = `Math.round`), and that is not a rounding convenience: his table is written in integers, so carried raw a perfectly ordinary 100.4 kg/m³ falls between «1–100» and «101–150» and is covered by neither — eleven one-wide holes in the densest part of the traffic, which the seed's own numbers would have created on day one. Rounding closes exactly those and closes nothing else (900.6 still finds no row), and it is also the number the VED sees, since every density on every screen in this system is printed with `Math.round`.

770. **Four more of the review's blockers, each a rule now.** (a) **The section drives the totals, and it comes first.** A `rastamojka` quote does not have a freight line that happens to be zero — it does not have a freight line, and the band must not appear on the sheet at all; `sectionParts(section)` is asked by the screen and by the seal. (b) **The freight zone is chosen, never inferred** — CN vs Kashgar is a 36-58 % difference and `from_city` is free text a seller typed on a phone, so `guessZone` is a pure PRE-SELECTION printed beside the seller's own words and the seal refuses `freight_zone_required` however the city reads. The zone options come from the tariff's own distinct zones, so a third route needs a row and no code. (c) **A band override and a discount are two different things** and stay apart all the way into the version: an override is the VED's judgement about the CARGO, a discount is a concession to this CLIENT, both are flagged and announced to the `finance.debt_override` holders (his own rule: «skidka berilganda biz bilishimiz kerak»), and only the second is the column phase D reads to withdraw the upsale right. (d) **The card's price is the VERSION, not `answer_*`** — phase A's `endRequest` writes the answer columns on every ending, so a request sealed and then ended another way would read back the later, emptier answer; the seal writes no `answer_*` at all and the card asks `currentSealFor`.

771. **The seal is one transaction, the version number comes OUT of it, and there is no re-open.** `UPDATE calc_requests SET current_version_no = current_version_no + 1 … WHERE id = $1 AND completed_at IS NULL RETURNING current_version_no` — no row means somebody else got there first and the answer is `already_closed`, so two people pressing «Muhrlash» in the same second cannot both write version 3. `getSetting('quote_valid_days')` is read BEFORE the transaction opens (a pooled call inside `db.transaction` deadlocks the pool), and validity is FROZEN onto the version, so shortening the setting never shortens a quote a client is already holding; expiry itself is decided at READ time, `issue_approvals`' rule, with nothing overnight to fall behind. **A correction is a NEW request** seeded from the sealed one and linked by `supersedes_request_id`, never a re-opening: clearing `completed_at` would re-arm the overdue sweep, the clock and the manual «Bajarildi» against a request that already has a locked price behind it — and it is exactly what an EXPIRED quote needs too, so there is one path and not two. The copy deliberately does NOT carry the confirmations across: a confirmation is a person saying «I have looked at these numbers», and they have not looked at these.

772. **The lock is real, or it is not a lock.** His law 2 says the price lands on the card and neither seller nor VED may change it — but `leads.quoted_amount` was an ordinary ✏️ field. The seal now WRITES the card's quote, both edit forms lose their quote inputs while a live version stands, and `updateLead`/`updateDeal` refuse a changed one (`quote_sealed`) — because a control removed from a screen while the action still accepts the field is hidden, not removed (round 70's rule). It is a CHANGE check and not a presence check, and #171 is why: these are replace-all forms, so the locked branch re-posts the sealed figures as hidden inputs and an ordinary save — a corrected phone number — must not become a refusal. `quoteLockedFor` is dynamically imported because `calc/workspace` reaches `crm/service` through `calc/service`, and it CATCHES `isServerBehind`: the lock is a safeguard, not a gate, and its absence must not take a card down on deploy morning.

773. **The model proposes words; it can never reach a number.** `proposeGoodsGrouping` fills labels, TNVED codes and a grouping, in batches of 200 (`planBatches`/`mergeProposals` — a batch that fails costs its own goods and not the whole file, an item no group claimed becomes its own low-confidence group rather than vanishing, and adding each batch's offset back is #171's positional-zip shape with money on the end). What it estimates lands in `ai_duty_pct`, which the arithmetic cannot name. Three fences, all cheap to lose in a refactor and therefore pinned as source shape in `tests/unit/ai-advisory.test.ts`: `pricing.ts` contains no `ai` identifier at all; the row the seal writes carries only `ai_groups_sealed`/`low_confidence_sealed`, counts for phase E and never an amount; and the database itself refuses — `rate_source` and `baza_source` allow `'dictionary'` and `'typed'` and **there is no `'ai'`**, so a prefill has nowhere to land however the call arrives. On top of that every group must be CONFIRMED by a person before the seal will take it, and changing a rate clears the confirmation, because the person who confirmed did not confirm these numbers. Red-proven by substituting `aiDutyPct` into the arithmetic: the tripwire and an integration test both go red. The proposal also claims its request (`ai_proposal_started_at`, released in `finally`) so two presses cannot both spend a model call, and `proposeGoodsGrouping` finally got the 60-second client timeout round 97's lesson never reached — an un-deadlined network call is the failure that looks like a hang.

774. **The defect the tests found, and it would have made the whole override useless.** `sealCalc` first loaded the plain draft, refused on its blockers, and only then applied the band override — so a load at a density his tariff cannot price (the 900-999 hole) was refused as `not_ready` before the override was ever considered, and the button whose entire purpose is to rescue exactly that load could never be pressed. `loadWorkspace(requestId, {overrideDensity, discountUsd})` now takes what the VED has typed but not yet sealed, so the screen PREVIEWS with the same call the seal validates with and the two cannot disagree. Two more found by looking rather than by testing: the group table needed 40rem with the measures on the primary row, and the main column is 490 px at 1280 — so **the customs figure, the one number the whole screen exists to produce, sat off the right edge on the widest screen the company owns**; the measures moved to the muted second line (round 88's answer to the same problem on the scanner) and the table came down to 22rem. And four house tripwires caught this round's own code before CI could: a phantom `bg-surface-2`, bare `w-…` on `.input` (`.input` carries `w-full` and wins on source order), `common.add`/`common.updated` in no bundle, and the production seed mentioning a demo warehouse NAME in a comment about a tariff zone — the zones are named by code and nowhere by city.

775. **Phase A's «Bajarildi» stays, and that is deliberate.** The dictionaries are born EMPTY, so on the morning this deploys nothing can be sealed at all — the seal needs a baza per item and a rate per code, and the company's knowledge of both is currently in one person's head and an Excel file. If the typed answer had been removed with the workspace's arrival, the queue would have had no way to answer anything until the dictionaries were filled. So the two coexist: `answer_*` keeps its 0085 meaning (a request with no version), the seal writes none of it, and the card reads the VERSION when there is one. The migration for phase C's client-facing sheet, phase D's upsale and phase E's calc-vs-actual all read `calc_versions`, which is why it carries the whole `breakdown` snapshot — every group with its rates, every item with its baza, every extra and the tariff row used — rather than a total somebody would later have to reconstruct.

776. **The owner closed the tariff's two holes the same night, and the table became contiguous.** Phase B shipped refusing 900-999 kg/m³ (`band_missing`) and 700 (`band_ambiguous`) rather than resolving either downward, and asked him three questions with the money attached. His answers — «1 sen aytgandek / 2 ozing togirla ketma ket qanday kelyabti shunga mosla / 3 shunday qolsim» — converge on one table: 900-999 takes the $320/$200 row, every band starts where the one before it ended plus one, and the step at 1000 stays a step. So «700–900» became **701–999**, 700 stays in «501–700», and the table now covers every whole kg/m³ from 1 upwards exactly once in both zones. Because the tariff has never run in production (0086 is unmerged), this is a correction to the SEED and not a dated superseding row — a «correction» dated over a table nobody has ever priced with would be archaeology, and the seed writes only into an empty table, so his server gets the settled shape on first deploy. **The engine's two refusals STAY**, and that is the point worth keeping: they are a property of the tariff a person may edit on `/admin/tarif` tomorrow, not of today's data, and they are why a hole typed next year will be a visible «bu zichlik uchun tarif yo'q» rather than a quietly cheaper invoice. The band table moved OUT of `scripts/seed.ts` into `wms/calc/tariff-seed.ts` because two things now need the same rows and must never disagree about them (#513): the seed that writes them, and the fence that proves they have no holes. That fence is the round's real dividend — it walks every whole density 1..1500 in both zones and asserts exactly one band claims each, plus the three densities his answers decided (700 → $300, 900/950/999 → $320, 1000 → per-kg) by name, so a later edit cannot quietly undo a decision he made with the numbers in front of him. Red-proven by restoring «700–900»: **198 densities become unpriceable** and 700 goes ambiguous. Two integration tests were rewritten with their reasons recorded rather than deleted — the one that asserted 950 refuses now asserts the SEEDED DATABASE prices it at $9,600 (which also proves the seed wrote what the module says), and the band-override test moved off 950 entirely, because once 950 prices itself the old test could no longer show the override had done anything: it now overrides a 50 kg/m³ load to 950 and asserts the price MOVED from $3,300 to $9,600 while `density` on the sealed version still reads 50.

777. **The shipped-code audit: NaN was a hole straight through all three layers, and the seal's own CHECK held it open.** Phase B was reviewed BEFORE code and never AS SHIPPED, so a six-lens adversarial pass was run over the commit (round 92b's precedent). Its headline is the defect the module exists to prevent, arriving by the one door nobody had shut: **`Number('1 000')` and `Number('abc')` are `NaN`, and NaN answers FALSE to every comparison a guard is made of** — `NaN < 0 || NaN > 100` is false, `NaN > 0` is false, `!(NaN >= 0)` is true. So a mistyped rate, baza, extra, discount or tariff price passed every range check in the services, `toFixed(2)` turned it into the string `'NaN'`, postgres stored it (NaN is a real `numeric` value there), and **`'NaN'::numeric >= 0` is TRUE**, so `calc_versions_total_check` — the last gate before a number becomes what the client was told — waved it through onto a LOCKED card. Measured, not argued: `totalsFor` with a NaN discount returned `{ok: true}` with a NaN total, from the file whose stated contract is that it never returns a number it had to invent. Fixed in all three layers, because any one of them alone is a guess about the others: `isNumber` in `pricing.ts` makes every entry point refuse `not_a_number`; `mustBeNumber` in the two services refuses `bad_number` before a write; and the migration gained `<> 'NaN'::numeric` on every money and rate column, which is the only comparison postgres has that excludes it (`x = x` does not — postgres defines NaN as equal to itself). `calc_groups` had no numeric CHECK at all and now has one.

778. **A sealed price stopped following a won lead, and #767 had already written the rule down.** `winLead` mints a DEAL carrying the lead's quote, `currentSealFor` finds a version by the card its request points at, and `rekeyLeadCalcRequests` filtered `openRequests` — so a SEALED request stayed on the lead and the new deal got the number with none of the lock. The ✏️ form on that deal rendered its editable branch, which means the quote became freely editable at exactly the moment it stops being a quote and becomes the invoice. Decision #767 item 11 states this rule («a sealed request follows a won lead — re-key closed ones too») and the code shipped without it: writing a rule down is not applying it. The filter is gone; every one of a lead's requests moves. The won lead keeps its own copy as history, and the deal — the live record — is the one that stays locked.

779. **Three more from the same pass.** (a) A **confirmation is about NUMBERS**, and only `setGroupRates` cleared it: changing an item's BAZA, or moving cargo into or out of a group, left the ✅ standing over a customs figure nobody had looked at, and the seal took it. `unconfirm()` is now called from both ends of a move and from every baza write — the same rule the rates path already had, applied to the other two things that change the same number. (b) **`pullRatesAction` took the rates from the BROWSER and stamped them `source: 'dictionary'`** — so a hand-posted call could write any rate at all and label it as the dictionary's word, which is precisely the provenance that column exists to record. The action now sends only which group; `pullRatesFromDictionary` reads the dictionary server-side and refuses `rates_not_in_dictionary` for a code it has never heard of. (c) The sealed panel printed «Yo'lkira $0.00» on a rastamojka quote — the module's own rule is that such a quote does not HAVE a freight line, and a zero on the sheet the client reads says the road was free. The panel now reads the parts from the VERSION's own section, so it shows what the quote WAS rather than what the request says today. Also fixed: the migration's two `DO` blocks tested `pg_constraint.conname` without `conrelid`, which is not unique across tables.

780. **The price book is keyed on the TNVED CODE, and that key is the whole design.** The obvious key for «what do we usually charge for this» is the product NAME, and it does not normalise: «Ayollar kurtkasi», «куртка жен.» and «women's jacket» are one thing and three strings, so two VED people asking the same question get two answers and neither knows. The code does normalise — it is written down, it is CONFIRMED by a person before anything can be sealed against it (phase B refuses a group whose code and rates nobody has looked at), and it is already the grain the customs side works in. So `calc_price_book` is (tnved_code, effective_date) UNIQUE, read exactly like `fx_rates` and the three phase-B dictionaries: newest row on or before the day, with **no earliest-row fallback** — a question asked about a day before the first row has no answer, and inventing one dates a price to before it existed. It stores the **CLIENT** price and not the sealed floor: a book filled from `calc_versions` would be a book of floors labelled as prices, and the seller reading it would quote the number the company must not go below. Red-proven both ways.

781. **An offer is the seller's price; the seal is only its floor.** `calc_offers` records what a seller actually told a customer, against the version that priced it — `client_price_usd`, the locale it went out in, the exact text, and `below_floor` computed against `calc_versions.total_usd`. Quoting below the floor is ALLOWED and flagged, never refused: the owner's law 4 makes the sealed number the floor, and a hard lock at the moment a seller is on the phone with a customer is a lock they route around by not using the screen. Phase D turns the flag into the upsale conversation. The offer text itself obeys three rules, each of which was a finding against the first design: (a) it never prints the sealed total, which is what the calculation COST and is the company's business; (b) it does not DECOMPOSE the price — `sealCalc` writes `freight_usd` and `freight_list_usd` from one value, so showing the parts hands the customer our list price and lets them subtract the discount we were willing to give; (c) **no emoji, ever** — the same text is drawn into a PDF with NotoSansSC and every emoji has glyph id 0 there, i.e. a hole in a customer's document with no error raised anywhere. MEASURED in the font file: U+2018 (what the Uzbek bundle uses) has a glyph; U+02BC and every emoji have glyph id 0. The per-unit lines are derived from the CLIENT price and never from the sealed one, or the two numbers on the sheet disagree the moment a seller quotes above the floor — which is the normal case.

782. **Delivery is reported honestly, and that needed a question asked before the send.** `notifyStaffTelegram` queues a row whether or not the recipient has a linked chat, and the drain settles an unlinked one as `muted / 'telegram not linked'` minutes later — which `notificationProblemCount` deliberately excludes, so nothing anywhere would ever have said the offer did not arrive. That is exactly how round 104's off-site backup went unnoticed for months. `recordOffer` therefore asks the drain's OWN predicate first (`telegram_links` with `status = 'linked'`) and returns `delivered`, and the screen prints «✅ Telegramga yuborildi» or «⚠ Telegram ulanmagan — matnni nusxalab oling» over a read-only textarea and a copy button. The seller is never left believing a message is in flight that nothing will ever send.

783. **The history is per-SECTION and per-CODE, and it refuses a per-product figure it cannot make exactly.** `totalsFor` zeroes the parts a section does not have, so `per_m3_usd` is FREIGHT alone on a yolkira quote, CUSTOMS alone on a rastamojka one and everything on a podklyuch one — printing the three in one «price per cube» column compares three different services, so every row carries its section as a chip. Customs IS per-product because `customsFor` runs per GROUP and the seal stores each group's own figure; freight is NEVER allocated onto a product, because it is computed once for the whole request from the whole request's density — measured, a 30 m³ mix of monitors and tiles lands in band 451-500 at $290/m³ while the monitors alone are band 1-100 at $110/m³, so allocating the mixed rate onto them overstates their freight 2.64×. And `groupPerUnit` refuses unless EVERY item in the group carries the measure: `groupMeasure` sums only the items that have one, so a three-item group where one item has a volume reports that item's volume as the group's and the division prints roughly three times the true rate. An OLD sealed row has no `volumeM3` on its items at all — the field was added to the snapshot in this phase — which reads as a missing measure and refuses, rather than dividing by whatever it finds.

784. **Two query shapes, both of them the rule stated somewhere else in this file.** (a) `quoteHistoryFor` uses EXISTS over `calc_groups` and not a JOIN: a request may hold several groups, so a join returns the version once PER GROUP and «the last five quotes» silently becomes «the last five group rows», which on a five-group consignment is one quote. EXISTS also lets the ORDER BY and the LIMIT stay in SQL, where a busy code's history does not have to be fetched whole to be cut to five (#432). (b) `lastQuotesByCode` — the workspace shows this beside every code on the screen, so one `quoteHistoryFor` per code would be a query per row on a list, the shape rounds 45, 68 and 108 each found saturating the one Node process. It is ONE query with `row_number() OVER (PARTITION BY code ORDER BY sealed_at DESC)`, the funnel's own per-stage cap (#74): a plain LIMIT over the union takes the newest N ACROSS all codes, so a busy code fills the list and a quiet one shows nothing. The inner DISTINCT matters too — a request may hold two groups carrying the same code, and without it that version is counted twice and one of the three slots prints the same quote again. Both red-proven.

785. **The monthly review is a NOTIFICATION on a daily clock, not a task and not a monthly cron.** A task needs one assignee and this is addressed to a set (`ved.docs` holders) — the DebtApproval pair's shape, #735's reasoning verbatim. And `0 6 1 * *` fires once: if the container is down that minute — a deploy, a restart, the morning the VPS is resized — the month is skipped with nothing anywhere to notice it. So the sweep runs DAILY and claims the month. The claim is one statement, `INSERT … ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE … WHERE value IS DISTINCT FROM the month RETURNING key`, so two overlapping sweeps on the same morning cannot both speak (0082's rule, red-proven: without the `setWhere` the second claim wins too). It is also **silent when nothing is stale** — the counts are the whole content of the message, so a zero is silence AND the month is left unclaimed, and next week's sweep may still find something and speak. The month is read in Asia/Tashkent, or a sweep at 06:00 UTC on the 1st announces the previous month.

786. **The PDF route carries an explicit door, because this app has no middleware at all.** Every `/api` route in this codebase gates itself, and a route that forgot to is how `/transit`, both batch documents and the partner screens each leaked (#721-726). `/api/calc/[versionId]/offer.pdf` therefore asks the SAME pair the button asks — `canWriteDeal`, plus `crm.leads` when the version belongs to a lead — so the sheet and the press cannot disagree about who may produce this. It renders the newest recorded OFFER and 404s when there is none, so the only way to get a sheet is to have made an offer: a route rendering `calc_versions.total_usd` would hand the customer the company's floor in a document. Pinned as source shape by `calc-offer-wire.test.ts`, whose negative assertions were each proven to bite by inserting the forbidden line — a source-shape test that cannot go red is a comment.

787. **A red proof that stayed green, and what it was actually measuring.** The NaN test on the offer price asserted `rejects.toThrow()` and passed with the engine's fence STRIPPED — because `calc_offers.client_price_usd` carries `> 0 AND <> 'NaN'::numeric` and the database refused it. Belt and braces is right, and the assertion was still worthless: a 23514 reaches the seller as a white page while a `CalcError` reaches them as a sentence, which is the whole of #472's rule. Re-anchored on the CODE (`bad_number` / `price_positive`) and only then did stripping the fence go red. Same lesson as #166 and the phase-B band proof, in its third costume: **when a red proof will not go red, the thing to look at is what else is refusing.**

788. **The PDF font cannot write Uzbek Cyrillic, and has not been able to for a year.** Found while looking at phase C's own offer sheet rather than at a failing test. MEASURED in `NotoSansSC-Regular.ttf`: **ў Ў қ Қ ғ Ғ ҳ Ҳ — all four of Uzbek Cyrillic's own letters — have glyph id 0**, together with thirty other characters inside the 0x400-0x45F range `BASE_CHARS` deliberately asks for. The subset requests them, gets nothing, and `drawText` draws `.notdef`: a blank box, with no error raised anywhere. Russian proper is unaffected (А-Яа-я and Ё all have glyphs), which is why it has never been noticed. It reaches TWO documents — the new offer sheet, and the **handover act**, which has been printing customer and receiver names for a year, i.e. people have signed acts with their own name missing from them. Fixed once, in `cjk-font.ts`, and consumed by both: `pdfTextCleaner()` transliterates the eight letters into the country's own Latin alphabet (Ў → `O'`, қ → `q`, …; the apostrophe is ASCII U+0027 because U+02BC, which the orthography actually specifies, is ALSO glyph 0 here) and drops anything else the font cannot draw. Dropping rather than drawing is the honest half: a name reaches these documents from the client book and from Telegram display names, an emoji in a display name is ordinary, and a gap reads as a gap while a box reads as corruption. `tests/unit/pdf-glyphs.test.ts` measures the font itself rather than asserting from what the block ranges suggest — including the finding as an assertion, so replacing the font turns that test red and says so. Red-proven. The awaited cleaner is a FACTORY returning a plain function, because the draw loops close over a mutable cursor and cannot be made async without rewriting them.

789. **The same defect as #778, one table over, found while designing phase D.** `recordOffer` denormalises the CARD onto `calc_offers` — `entity_type` and `entity_id` — so a card can list its own offers in one indexed query rather than joining back through the version and the request. `rekeyLeadCalcRequests` moved `calc_requests` and nothing else. So the moment a lead is won: the request follows, `currentSealFor` finds the version and the price stays locked (that is #778's fix working), while `offersFor('deal', dealId)` comes back EMPTY and what the seller actually promised the customer disappears from the only card that still exists — at exactly the moment a quote becomes an invoice. MEASURED against the shipped tree, not argued: the integration test was written first and was red. Round 110 wrote «fixing a rule in one place is not applying it» about #767 and then this round demonstrated the sentence again, which is why the fence is DERIVED rather than listed: `tests/unit/calc-rekey.test.ts` reads the schema for every `calc_*` table declaring both entity columns and asserts `rekeyLeadCalcRequests` re-keys each one, so a third such table turns it red on the day it is added. Source-shape and comment-stripped (#725 — my own prose about a table must not satisfy a fence about code). Both halves red-proven. Also worth stating: `crm_activities` carries the same pair and is deliberately NOT moved — a lead's lenta is that lead's history, and the deal has its own.

790. **Phase C leaked the client price to the VED on four surfaces, against the owner's own law 4.** Found by the phase-D design review and then verified by reading the seeded matrix, not taken on trust. `DEAL_WRITE_PERMISSIONS = ['crm.leads', 'ved.docs', 'clients.manage']` — `ved.docs` is in it deliberately (DEALS.md #2: the VED recalculates jobs), so `canWriteDeal` answers TRUE for the customs manager, and phase C put exactly that gate on four things: `/api/calc/[versionId]/offer.pdf`, the card panel's offer list, the offer FORM itself, and `/hisoblash/narxlar`'s «Цена клиенту» line. Law 4 says «**VED never sees upsale**», and upsale is client price minus the sealed floor — a number the VED computed themselves. So every one of those four handed them the subtraction.

791. **`upsaleScopeFor` keys on `finance.reports`, and NOT on round 91's `seesAllMoney`.** `seesAllMoney` is `finance.manage || clients.manage`, and **`ved_manager` holds `finance.manage`** — so the obvious predicate answers TRUE for precisely the person the law excludes. Red-proven: swapping the two turns the role matrix test red on the VED. Verified matrix: `finance.reports` = super_admin, admin, accountant, which is law 4's «owner + accountant» written out, with no new permission code (#170). It is not a hierarchy but **two views of one row**, because law 10 pulls the other way («sellers: prices only, no cost breakdown»): the VED reads the cost side and never a client price; the seller reads the client prices — that IS law 10's drift control — and never the floor. `scope` is a REQUIRED argument on `quoteHistoryFor`, not an optional one defaulting to `all`: an optional gate fails OPEN, and making it required turned every caller into a compile error that named itself. Under `own` the WHOLE derived family goes (`totalUsd`, `perM3Usd`, `perKgUsd`, the group's customs) — nulling the total alone leaves the floor one multiplication away from the volume printed on the same row — and the card LINK goes with it, because `/bitimlar/[id]` prints `calc-seal-total` and has no ownership gate of its own. The test is BEHAVIOURAL over every seeded role rather than a restatement of the predicate: the exclusion is a property of a matrix the owner edits with checkboxes, so the day he ticks `finance.reports` for the VED, the test says so in his vocabulary.

792. **The same predicate found a SECOND defect in the opposite direction, and only a browser saw it.** With the fix in, I logged in as each seeded role and read the four surfaces. The VED was correctly shut out — and so was the **buxgalter**, who is the one person law 4 names besides the owner («the accountant pays the upsale out to the seller»). `/hisoblash/narxlar` had gated on `canWriteDeal || ved.docs`, and an accountant holds none of `crm.leads`, `ved.docs` or `clients.manage`: measured, they were redirected to the home screen, and the offer PDF answered 403. So phase C had built the accountant's own screen and locked the accountant out of it. **A permission fix is only half-verified until somebody who is NOT an admin has opened the screen** — the same lesson round 43 recorded by logging in as the warehouse operator instead of reading the gate. Both doors are now law 4 itself (`upsaleScopeFor(actor) !== 'none'`, with `ved.docs` beside it on the history screen for law 10), which is strictly wider than the old door by exactly `finance.reports`. Re-measured across four roles: VED — sheet 403, client price hidden, cost side visible; seller — client prices visible, the whole cost family and the card link gone; buxgalter — both halves and the sheet; warehouse operator — redirected. The fence had to be fixed too: it read the route's raw text and my own sentence explaining that `canWriteDeal` is gone satisfied a `not.toContain('canWriteDeal')`, which is #725 wearing a negative assertion. It strips comments now.

**Stated to the owner and NOT fixed here:** `/bitimlar/[id]` gates on `canWriteDeal` with no ownership test, so a determined seller can open a colleague's deal card by URL and read the sealed floor there. That is a CRM access round, not a line in the VED module.

793. **The upsale is DERIVED and never stored; only its permission and its payment are facts.** It is `client_price_usd − calc_versions.total_usd`, and both parents are immutable — a version is never updated after it is written, a correction is a NEW request through `supersedes_request_id`, and an offer names its own `version_id` so the floor it was measured against cannot move under it. Writing the difference down could therefore only ever create a way for it to disagree with itself. What genuinely cannot be derived is stored: **whether the promise was allowed** (`approved_at`) and **whether the money has been handed over** (`payout_expense_id`, NULL being the whole pay-twice fence). Migration **0088**; ledger must reach **89**.

794. **`payableOffersSql()` is the one home, and `payUpsale` embeds it rather than trusting the ids that were ticked.** Five questions, each a rule from elsewhere in the module. *One payable per JOB* — `calc_offers` has no unique key and re-offering is the designed workflow (the seller picks a language and presses again), so without `row_number() OVER (PARTITION BY request_id ORDER BY offered_at DESC)` every re-quote is a second commission on one sale; the rank is per REQUEST and not per card, because 0085 deliberately dropped «one open request per card» and a client legitimately carries several jobs. *On the request's current version*, or a corrected job pays on the figure it was corrected away from. *On a request nobody superseded* — a correction is a new request, and the old promise is not the one that stands. *Released* — a below-floor promise no admin allowed is not a promise. *Eligible and positive* — a loss is not a commission. Red-proven three ways: strip the rank and a re-quote pays twice; strip the supersedes clause and a corrected job still pays; strip the discount clause and law 4 is gone.

795. **Two things concede, and `freight_usd <> freight_list_usd` is not one of them — testing it is a trap in BOTH directions.** Traced through the code, not assumed: `sealCalc` passes `freight.listUsd` in as `freightUsd` and writes `freight_list_usd` from the same value, so on a yolkira or podklyuch quote the two are one expression and the test finds a freight concession NEVER. And on a **rastamojka** quote `totalsFor` zeroes the freight the section does not have while `freight_list_usd` is still written from the resolved band — so the test fires on every customs-only job and would strip the upsale from a whole section of the business. What actually concedes is `discount_usd`, and a band override that LOWERS the freight: the tariff is monotone in density (a unit test walks 1..1500), so an override below the real density buys a cheaper band, while one above it is the VED correcting an overstated m³ and charging MORE, which concedes nothing. A NULL density with an override set counts as a concession — a money rule fails closed.

796. **The payout is an `expenses` row in a MANDATORY dedicated category, and the amount is derived server-side.** Routing through `expenses` gives `accountBalances`, `cashFlow`, `profitAndLoss`, `/accounting/expenses` and `voidExpense` for free, and `employee_id` is the one column in the money schema that names a user as the subject of money. But `generateRecurring`'s idempotence slot is exactly `(category_id, expense_date, employee_id, warehouse_id)` with no discriminator, and a template that finds a live row on its slot counts as **skipped** — so a commission paid to a seller out of «Oyliklar» would silently cancel that seller's salary that month, in the P&L, in the till and on the home counter. Hence `upsale_expense_category_id` is a required setting and `payUpsale` refuses `upsale_category_unset` rather than guessing. The ACCOUNTANT picks which jobs, the till, the currency and the day; the SERVER sums those jobs' upsale and writes that, because a typed figure is how a screen says «$340 paid» while $200 leaves the till. A partial payment is fewer ticks, never a smaller number.

797. **The paired CHECK caught my own two-step write, which is what it is for.** `calc_offers_payout_pair_check` says paid is all four columns or none (0054's idiom). The first `payUpsale` claimed the offers first and stamped `payout_expense_id` in a second UPDATE, leaving three of four set in between — postgres refused, in a test, before anyone could be paid on a half-written row. The rewrite reads the payable set and its sum BEFORE the transaction, inserts the expense with that amount, and then claims all four columns in ONE statement whose `FROM` is the fragment itself; short by a row or short by a cent and the whole transaction rolls back, because the amount already written on the expense is no longer the right amount. **A CHECK that only ever fires in your own tests has still done its job.**

798. **The client-money question is three states, not one, and the deferral rule was given a home rather than a copy.** «Has the client paid» read alone is wrong: `charges − payments` is 0 for a client who has settled AND for one nobody has invoiced, and a client with no ledger rows has no row at all, so the offer falls out of every bucket and is never paid, silently. So: `no_invoice` (less charged on this deal than was quoted) → `awaiting_payment` (charged, still owing) → `payable`. Collection uses `balance − deferred`, the HANDOVER gate's own arithmetic — a commission gate stricter than the gate that already released the cargo leaves sellers unpaid on goods the company has handed over. `liveDeferralWhere()` was extracted in `finance/service.ts` so the handover gate and the payout read one predicate (#513), and `balancesForClients` answers for many clients in two queries rather than two per row (#432).

799. **The below-floor lock goes on the PROMISE, not on the record — and that reconciles law 4 with #781.** Law 4 says below-floor is admin-only; #781 argued a hard door in front of a seller with a customer on the phone is a door they route around by not using the screen. Both are right about different things, because in this codebase **the offer row IS the customer document**: `recordOffer` builds the text and pushes it to the seller's chat, and the PDF route renders the same numbers. So the row is always written — the flag is how the owner sees who is discounting — and what is locked is everything that reaches the customer. Until `approved_at` is stamped: no Telegram text, no PDF link, not the card's price, and never payable. The seller sees «ruxsat kutilmoqda» and no copy button. A reason is mandatory, exactly as a discount's is. An admin pressing the price themselves is recorded and released in one step, and `offered_by` stays the SELLER — crediting the approver would blank the seller's row in the report that measures them, precisely on the hard deals. Releasing is single-shot and the claim IS the UPDATE (0082's rule): releasing is what sends the customer the message, so two admins pressing in the same second must not both send it.

800. **A released offer writes the CLIENT price onto the card — and that quietly broke the quote lock, which the design did not see.** `sealCalc` writes the floor to `quoted_amount`, and every revenue surface reads that column (`funnelReport`, five places in `salesAnalytics`, `salesSnapshot`, the board's money line), so today the CRM reports the company's own cost as its revenue. Law 4 says the client pays the VED price plus the upsale, so the card must carry what the customer pays. **But `quoteLockedFor` returns the SEALED total and `updateLead`/`updateDeal` refuse any save whose posted amount differs from it — and the locked ✏️ form re-posts exactly what it renders (#171).** So writing the client price onto the card while the lock still answered «the floor» would have refused EVERY later save on a quoted card, for ever. Found by reading the lock, not by a test; then red-proven both ways. `quoteLockedFor` now asks `releasedPriceFor` first: **the card and the lock have to agree about which number is the one nobody may change.** A pending below-floor promise is not that number, because the customer has not been told it.

801. **`mayApproveBelowFloor` is `finance.reports`, and the alarm goes to the same set.** `finance.debt_override` is the obvious grant and is held by the logist, every warehouse manager and every sales manager — so a below-floor alarm carrying a client price and a margin would go to the Kashgar warehouse and to competing sellers. That is a leak wearing an alarm's clothes. `finance.reports` resolves to super_admin, admin and accountant, which is exactly the set `upsaleScopeFor` answers `all` for: **the people who may allow a concession are the people who may see what it costs**, and `approverIds()` asks the same predicate of the roster so the door and the audience cannot drift apart. `CalcBelowFloor` sits in MUTE_GROUPS.alerts, not operations: nothing has been said to the customer yet and the seller is standing in front of one.

802. **`/upsale` is two shapes from one query, and the commonest status is not painted red.** A nine-column table at 360 px is wider than the viewport, and a document wider than its viewport rescales the whole page (#400) — on the screen the sellers read their own pay on. So a LIST below `md` and a table from `md`, measured in the browser at both widths (document = viewport at each). Two things came out of looking at it rather than from any test: the pay form listed every payable job that the table below already showed, so it is FOLDED and the default view answers «what did we earn» while paying is a deliberate act; and `payable` was a brand-red chip, which made the commonest row on the screen look like an alarm and hid the one row that is one — «the client has not paid». Payable is neutral now and warn is reserved for what needs chasing.

803. **PROCESS, mine: #738's trap in a new hat — I chained two gates and read only the second.** The intermediate commit `7c04b0b` went to CI with two unused-import lint errors, and CI is where I learned it. What I ran was a single backgrounded block, `pnpm lint … ; pnpm vitest run …`, and then grepped the combined output for `Test Files|Tests` — so eslint's «✖ 2 problems» was in the output I asked for and not in the lines I looked at, and the block's exit code was vitest's, not eslint's. #738 recorded exactly this about `pnpm typecheck | tail` reporting the TAIL's status, and the shape survived being written down because the second time it wore a different pipe. **A gate whose failure you have to go looking for is not a gate.** From here: gates run with `&&` so the first failure stops the block, and the grep names each gate's own failure marker rather than a shared summary line.

804. **Phase E's money comparison is CUSTOMS ONLY, and the design's version could not have shipped.** The spec asks «hisob vs haqiqat» and the obvious reading is `calc_versions.total_usd` against the landed cost. Traced: `freight_usd` comes from `freightFor`, whose own docstring reads «the list price of the road, before any concession», while `cost_allocations.amount_usd` is what the truck cost us. **Price minus cost is margin.** On the owner's own tariff a 30 m³ / 200 kg/m³ cn load quotes $4,800 from row `[151,200,160,100]` while the road bought costs roughly $2,700 — **+78 %, permanently, on a calculation executed exactly as quoted**. Against any threshold at all, every sealed version trips for ever and the list is worthless by the second week. Customs is a different kind of number: `customsFor` = duty + VAT + fee is money payable to the STATE, and the accountant types the same payment into the cost grid — two measurements of one fact, so a gap is an error. It is also where the model's only money-bearing proposal lands (`ai_duty_pct`), which is what closes his loop: **the AI proposes the duty → the duty drives the customs figure → calc-vs-actual on customs is where confirming it blind turns into money.** Margin already has a home in `dealProfit`. Freight gets a BAND CHECK instead — `freightFor` re-run at the density the cargo actually arrived at, reported as «quoted band 151-200 at $160/m³, cargo arrived at 96 kg/m³ → band 1-100». Deterministic, free, and it is the decision the VED actually makes.

805. **The join lives at the RECEIPT grain, has ONE writer, and an `auto` link never scores anybody.** Nothing in the system could answer «which prixod was this quote about»: `calc_requests` names a lead or a deal, `receipts` names a deal, and a deal carries many of both — 0085 deliberately dropped «one open request per card», and `dealFor` sends every repeat client to their newest OPEN deal while no seeded stage carries a cargo trigger, so one open deal alive for months with several quotes on it is the ordinary shape. A door that stamped «this deal has exactly one sealed calculation» would hang a quarter's prixods on one April quote and report +1200 % against the person who priced it correctly. So `receipts.calc_request_id` (0089), written only by `stampCalcLink` in `calc/link.ts`, called from `createReceipt`, `linkReceipt` and `sealCalculation` — **`createReceipt` too, which writes `deal_id` on its own INSERT and never goes through `linkReceipt`**, so the receive wizard's deal picker (used by exactly the person who knows which job the cargo belongs to) would otherwise have been the one door that bypassed the join. Two guards: exactly one sealed non-superseded request on the deal, and the prixod's `confirmed_at` inside `[requested_at, valid_until]` — the quote's own stated life, so the window is the business's answer and not a number invented here. And `auto` is a SUGGESTION: `measurableLinkSql` demands `calc_link_confirmed_at IS NOT NULL`, so until a person presses «Ha, shu yuk» nothing is measured. A guess that silently scores a person is worse than no number.

806. **A correction adopts its predecessor's cargo at SEAL time, and the measurement disagrees with the commission about an abandoned one.** Re-pointing inside `recalcFromSealed` was refused after reading it: that function inserts a request with no `currentVersionNo` and no version at all, so the cargo would hang off a PRICELESS request — permanently, if the VED then hands the job back through `endRequest('returned')`. `sealCalculation` runs the UPDATE after the version's INSERT, where a price exists by construction. The accuracy query's «not superseded» clause is therefore NOT the one `payableOffersSql()` uses and deliberately so (`measurableRequestSql`, which additionally requires the successor to have SEALED): money must stop paying on a promise the moment a correction is opened, because a superseding request means the old promise no longer stands; a measurement must NOT drop the shipment, because the cargo was priced, it arrived, and the old figure is the only one that ever existed for it. Sharing the clause would have made an abandoned correction a way to disappear from the accuracy table.

807. **A ✅ is a record of what stood on the screen, and there are TWO writers that must clear it.** The owner's question is «ko'rmasdan tasdiqlagan» — did somebody confirm a number the model invented without looking? It cannot be answered by re-deriving today's warnings, because the dictionaries move: a month later the same code has a dictionary rate and the question has silently become a different one. So `calc_groups.confirmed_warnings` + `confirm_via` are written AT the press (0089), and the seal carries `warned_groups` / `ai_blind_groups` / `ai_rate_taken_groups` away with it. The trap is that `unconfirm()` is not the only clear: **`setGroupRates` INLINES `confirmedBy: null, confirmedAt: null` in its own UPDATE** and never calls it, so a fix aimed at the helper alone leaves `calc_groups_confirm_pair_check` raising 23514 on the most-pressed button in the workspace. Both were fixed and both have their own red proof — proof 2 stays GREEN when only `unconfirm` is stripped, which is the whole reason there are two. `pullBazasFromDictionary` gained the clear it never had: it fills every blank baza in one pass and a baza is one of the numbers the tick was about.

808. **The warning rule needs the `dictionary !== null` half or the first list is 100 % of the company.** «Rate not from the dictionary» reads naturally as `rate_source = 'typed'`, and that is TRUE of every group there is: `0086` and `0087` ship the dictionaries EMPTY, so on deploy morning nobody has ever priced anything from one and typing a rate is the only thing a person can do. The rule is «the dictionary HAD an answer and a person typed something else». Same for the baza. `ai_low_confidence` is a three-clause conjunction — the model actually proposed this group (`mergeProposals` mints the ORPHAN group, the cargo the model did not place, with confidence `'low'` and `aiProposed: false`), it said so itself with low confidence, and the dictionary could not have corrected it. That third fence also corrected a live defect: `lowConfidenceSealed` counted confidence alone, so «how much of this was still the model's» was inflated by a group nobody's model ever touched, on every calculation that has one. And `ai_rate_taken` — `ai_duty_pct IS NOT NULL ∧ rate_source = 'typed' ∧ duty = ai_duty` — is literally his sentence («ai bergan taklif bilan narx berib yuborganda») reading a column that had existed since 0086 with no reader at all.

809. **`ON DELETE SET NULL` and a multi-column CHECK cannot coexist, and I wrote the same bug twice.** The design asked for a biconditional between `calc_request_id` and `calc_link_source`; it was refused after MEASURING it in postgres — `ON DELETE SET NULL` is an internal `UPDATE ONLY receipts SET calc_request_id = NULL` touching the FK column ALONE, so the constraint fails 23514 and the parent DELETE aborts. Six integration files delete `calc_requests` in cleanup and all six would have started silently leaving rows behind (#183). The replacement written in its place — «confirmed_at IS NULL OR request_id IS NOT NULL» — has the identical shape and was caught by its own test the first time it ran. **Any constraint spanning the FK column and a sibling does this.** So the state «confirmed, but the calculation was deleted» is legal, and it costs nothing: every reader asks for both columns, so an orphaned stamp scores nothing and shows nothing. `calc-actuals.integration.test.ts` deletes a `calc_request` with a linked prixod and asserts the delete SUCCEEDS — the standing guard, because the tempting fix next year is to add the constraint back.

810. **The control screen needs a THIRD door, and reusing either of the two would have leaked in opposite directions.** `upsaleScopeFor` answers `'none'` for `ved_manager` (no `finance.reports`) — right for a screen carrying a client price and exactly wrong here, because the VED is the person being measured and has to see the number to act on it; and it answers `'own'` for every `sales_manager` (they hold `crm.leads`), which would put the company's landed customs costs on a seller's screen, against law 10's «sellers: prices only, no cost breakdown». Round 91's `seesAllMoney` is `finance.manage || clients.manage`, which admits the VED to everybody's row rather than their own. So `calcControlScopeFor`: `finance.reports` → all, `ved.docs` → own, everybody else → none. No new permission code (#170), so the audience is a property of the matrix he edits with checkboxes — which is why the test enumerates every seeded role rather than asserting the predicate's shape. **Reach without touching nav:** the accountant cannot open `/hisoblash` at all (it redirects on `ved.docs`), so the link is on `/hisoblash/narxlar`, whose door they already pass, as well as on the queue's header for the VED. #792 shipped a screen with its own audience locked out one round ago; this time all four roles were opened in a real browser and the e2e asserts each answer.

811. **Six refusals, and the screen prints ⚠ and a reason where a number would be a lie.** Every one of these would otherwise read as a −100 % saving or a +∞ overrun against somebody's name: `customs_by_client` (the client cleared it through their own firm), `section_has_no_customs`, `unconverted` (`amount_usd IS NULL` — `recomputeEntry` returns before allocating, so a missing FX rate reads as a saving), `unallocated` (converted but nothing written — a `direct_to_client` entry filed against a client who owns none of these boxes), `no_actual_yet` (nobody has typed it — a wait, not an error) and `no_actual_cost`, which NAMES the cost types it found so the mapping is discoverable rather than silently wrong. The mapping itself is DATA (`calc_customs_cost_type_codes`), because a cost type he mints on `/admin/cost-types` gets a `t_…` code that is matched by nothing. Nothing about the comparison is stored: `voidCostEntry` deletes allocations and a late FX rate re-prices an entry, so a figure frozen the night the truck landed is the lie the screen would repeat for ever.

812. **Two clocks, because one guarantees an empty table.** Coverage and the warnings run on SEALED-AT (the current month). The accuracy table runs on ARRIVED-AT plus a settle window (`calc_actual_settle_days`, 7), over a trailing 90 days. The road is a 10.5-day floor and about 17 days typically (`map-data.ts`: 144-168 h + 24-72 border + 36-48), and a quote may stand 30 days before the cargo even ships — so on a single sealed-at clock the accuracy table is empty until roughly the 18th of any month, every month.
