Each section says which clock it is on, in its own header.

813. **Found by LOOKING at the screen, not by a test: the completeness gate hid the very error the round exists to show.** `cargo_incomplete` used `compareQuote`'s worst-of-both-measures, which is right for the question that function was written for («should somebody ring the client») and wrong for this one. Cargo that arrives at the quoted 30 m³ weighing 2,900 kg instead of 5,400 is not half a shipment — it is all of it, at a density the VED got wrong, which is precisely what this screen is for. On the first screenshot that row read «⚠ Solishtirib bo'lmaydi: Yukning hammasi kelmagan» and the money comparison never printed. Missing cargo is a VOLUME shortfall; a weight-only one is a mis-estimate and the band check below it names it. Same shape as #92's lesson: a rule that is right about the common case and silently wrong about the case the business has.

814. **E1 ships and E2 is gated on data that does not exist yet, stated numerically.** The suspicious rules the spec asks for stand on dictionaries and a price book that ship EMPTY (`0086`/`0087` contain zero INSERTs), so `rate_off_dictionary`, `baza_off_dictionary` and `all_typed` would be true of 100 % of groups and `floor_above_book` cannot fire at all. E2 — the history-dependent rules, the per-worker ranking, the workspace sidebar's actuals column and the digest — waits for **≥20 sealed versions with confirmed links and settled cargo, and a non-empty `calc_price_book`**. **No Telegram is minted in E1**: with empty dictionaries a push would either say nothing or name every calculation every morning, and this codebase has paid four times for a channel that stops being read. CUT entirely and owed to him as an explicit «yo'q»: **per-TNVED calc-vs-actual** — a calc group is a CODE and a receipt lot is a NAME in zh/ru, they do not join, and that is phase C's whole design note — and **the nightly AI pass**, which `gsr_ai_reader`'s allowlist cannot reach, which `ai_questions.user_id NOT NULL` makes unauditable on a schedule, and which costs ~$320/month for an opinion the arithmetic already gives deterministically.

815. **PROCESS, mine, #430 in a new costume: an empty replacement string is not a reversible edit.** My red-proof harness strips a line by replacing it with `""` and restores by replacing `""` back — and `s.replace("", line, 1)` inserts at **index 0**. Two source files came back with a stray line welded onto their first import, and the `grep -c` I used to check the restore answered «1» for both, because the line WAS present, just not where it belonged. Typecheck caught it; the check I ran did not. From here a strip replaces with a distinct marker (`// STRIPPED n`) and never with nothing, and the restore asserts the marker is there before putting the line back. Seventeen red proofs, every one by string edit, none by `git checkout`.

816. **The audit of E1 as shipped: five lenses, 25 candidates, and a BLOCKER that would have made the whole round render empty.** Phase B's shipped-code audit found NaN through three layers; this one found that **`arrived_at` could never be set**. The `arrival` CTE asked for `to_status = 'in_stock'` into a UZ warehouse — and unloading NEVER writes that: `landedStatus` in `unload.ts` is `ready_for_pickup` at a warehouse of type `customs` or `distribution`, and in Uzbekistan every destination is one of the two. MEASURED on real rows: of every movement into a UZ warehouse, `unload_scan` is `ready_for_pickup` 79 times and `in_stock` zero, so the predicate matched walk-in receipts alone. `settledFor(null, …)` is false for ever, so section 4 — the comparison the round exists for — would have printed «hali solishtiradigan narsa yo'q» on every truck, for months, with nothing on screen to say why. The rule already existed, correct and commented, in `documents/arrivals.ts`: causes IN (`unload_scan`, `undocumented_transfer`, `found_here`) and `from IS DISTINCT FROM to`. **I restated it instead of importing it**, which is the #513 failure the rest of this round is about; `ARRIVED_ON_A_TRUCK` is exported now and the SQL is the same sentence. The from/to clause is MEASURED redundant under those causes (0 of 234 rows) and kept anyway, so the two rules read alike — stated in the comment, because a guard that cannot currently fire is a claim somebody must be able to check.

817. **`linked` fanned out over the company's whole history to produce 200 rows.** `since` and `limit` bounded only the outer `calc_versions` scan; the `linked` CTE took EVERY receipt that had ever carried a confirmed link, and `arrival`, `entries`, `scoped`, `money` and `health` all built from it — walking `box_movements` and `cost_allocations` over every year the business has. EXPLAIN ANALYZE on a fixture selecting ONE output row: a seq scan of `receipts`, 185 lots, 100 boxes and 122 movements, none of it filtered. A `want` CTE now computes the admissible request ids first and every CTE joins it. The same lens found the three correlated `(SELECT count(*) FROM scoped …)` in the select list — a materialised CTE has no index, so that is a scan per output row, three times over, the #152 shape — now one `scoped_health` aggregate joined once. **Also from the availability lens, and the more dangerous one:** `stampCalcLink` filters `calc_requests` on `(entity_type, entity_id)`, which had NO index at all, from inside `createReceipt`'s transaction — a sequential scan on the warehouse's most-pressed button, holding a transaction open while it ran. 0089 has never deployed, so the index went into it.

818. **The link BUTTONS were gated on the page's audience and never on «own», so a VED could erase the measurement of a colleague.** Every list on `/hisoblash/nazorat` is scoped — `calcActuals`, `calcCoverage`, `linkSuggestions`, `warnedGroups` all take it — and the three actions asked one question, `calcControlScopeFor(actor) !== 'none'`, then passed a receipt id to services that re-derived nothing about whose calculation it was. **A door that filters what you see and not what you press is not a door.** `scope` is now a REQUIRED argument on `confirmCalcLink` and `setCalcLink` (the compiler named every caller, which is what required arguments are for) and `assertMine` refuses `not_mine` on BOTH ends of a re-point — clearing somebody else's confirmed link is the more damaging half, because it silently removes them from the measurement. An UNSEALED request passes: nobody is measured by it yet. And `confirmCalcLink` now re-proves the request against the receipt's own deal exactly as `setCalcLink` does — the ✓ writes the same fact, and a prixod re-filed between the render and the tap is when a stale suggestion is most likely on screen.

819. **`linkReceipt`'s RE-FILE branch kept the calc link; only its DETACH branch cleared it.** The detach branch spells out why — «a link left standing on a detached prixod would go on measuring a job it is no longer part of» — and the attach branch has the identical problem and wrote `deal_id` alone. `stampCalcLink` cannot repair it: its UPDATE is guarded by `calc_request_id IS NULL`, so a link a person had CONFIRMED survives the move, the old deal's calculation goes on being scored by cargo that left it, and the new deal's quote is measured by nothing. Two lenses found it independently and both measured it. Same commit: `recalcFromSealed` copied every group field except `ai_proposal`, so `unchangedFromProposal` answered false for every group of a correction and `ai_blind_groups` was permanently 0 on exactly the calculations somebody had already had to redo; and `confirmAllGroups`, which E1 turned from one `UPDATE … RETURNING` into a loop, is back inside a transaction — the per-group VALUES need a statement each, the atomicity did not have to go with them.

820. **A setting I shipped with no reader, in the same round I refused to mint one.** `calc_customs_deviation_pct` = 15 went into `SETTING_DEFAULTS` with a docstring and a description in all four bundles, so it renders as a live control on `/admin/settings` — and `grep` found exactly one hit, its own declaration. That is 0064's `tg_peer_index` mistake, which #814 explicitly avoided for `suspicious_notified_at` twenty lines earlier. The screen was colouring the customs gap on its SIGN, so a 0.4 % overrun looked exactly as alarming as a 60 % one. `customsOffThreshold` reads the owner's own number now and only a gap past it takes a colour.

821. **Two ways a bill goes MISSING, and the second one needed the batch.** `unallocated` as first written could never fire: the `entries` CTE reaches a cost entry only THROUGH `cost_allocations`, so «mapped entries exist but nothing summed» is unreachable — measured, every allocation on this database is positive. The real case is the opposite shape: an entry that wrote NO allocation rows at all is invisible to every CTE, and both ways of getting there make the actual read LOWER than the truth, i.e. put the VED in the wrong on a bill they had nothing to do with. A `scoped` CTE sees them without the allocation table — receipt scope for both, plus **batch scope for the unconverted half**, because round 29's agreed method puts shared rastamojka on the export truck as ONE batch-scope row and restricting the CTE to receipt scope hid the commonest customs bill there is whenever its FX rate had not arrived. Only the unconverted half at batch scope: a CONVERTED batch entry that allocated nothing to our boxes is not an error, a truck carries several clients. `entriesFound` had to learn about them too, or the ladder answers «not entered yet» about a bill sitting on the prixod — a wait and an error are different sentences.

822. **`coalesce(customs_by_client, false)` is a two-state reading of a three-state column.** `effectiveCustoms` is the system's one definition and says so: «`false` on the receipt is an answer, `null` is silence» and silence means the TRUCK's answer applies. `receipts.customs_by_client` is nullable with no default while `batches.customs_by_client` is NOT NULL — so «mijoz o'z firmasi bilan» set once on the batch card, which is exactly what the batch-level control exists for, was invisible here and the row was scored against a rastamojka we never paid. Restated as SQL through the departed-batch movements, with both halves red-proven separately: the truck's answer being read, and the prixod's explicit answer still winning over it.

823. **The settle gate ran in JavaScript after `LIMIT 200`, and the two clocks run in opposite directions.** A settled row is by construction an OLD seal — the road is a ten-day floor, about seventeen typical, plus up to a month of quote validity — while `ORDER BY sealed_at DESC LIMIT n` keeps the NEWEST. Above roughly eight seals a day the two sets stop overlapping, so the accuracy table would print «hali solishtiradigan narsa yo'q» under a coverage line reporting a full month of work: the «we have no errors» / «we have no data» confusion the section order was designed to prevent, arriving through the back door. The gate is a SQL clause before the LIMIT now (`settledOnly`), and the page asks for it. Also from the same lens and fixed: `cargo_incomplete` and `link_implausible` were keyed on VOLUME alone, and a rastamojka quote needs no volume at all — measured, both guards were dead for that whole section, so one 300 kg prixod against a 5,000 kg quote scored −94 % in green. Volume when there is one, weight when there is not.

824. **PROCESS, mine, twice more in one session.** (a) The first wiring test for the tariff-as-of fix INSERTED a dated tariff row — CONFIGURATION for the whole installation — and vitest runs test FILES in parallel, so it repriced every seal in `calc-seal` and `calc-offer` while it held: ten failures in files this round never touched. Rewritten to write nothing global. (b) The no-rate currency it used was **`AED`, which another test file creates** — green here, red on a fresh database, because vitest orders files by a duration cache and «a currency somebody else made» is a coin toss, not a fixture (#380). It mints its own now and deletes it LAST, after the cost entries that reference it: deleting it earlier threw in `afterAll`, which fails the FILE while every test still reads green — which is how the leftover survived the first run unnoticed. And (c) a refinement of #815: a red proof's strip marker must be UNIQUE in the file, not merely present. `s.replace(marker, original)` on a marker that appears twice welded a duplicate guard into `link.ts`; typecheck caught it, my `grep -c` did not.

825. **A setting nobody could set: `upsale_expense_category_id` needed a PICKER, not a text box.** Phase D made the payout's category mandatory and stopped there, because `SETTING_DEFAULTS` renders every key on `/admin/settings` and the row therefore exists. It does — as a mono input asking for a **uuid**, and no screen in this application prints an expense category's id. So the one control standing between the accountant and «to'lash» was a box the owner had no way to fill, and the refusal it produces (`upsale_category_unset`) would have read as a broken button rather than as a missing answer. His own report: «bunday narsa yo'qku sozlamalarda». The fix follows `crm_calc_stage`'s precedent exactly — a setting that names ONE row is chosen on the screen that owns the concept, not by typing an id — so the picker is a fold on `/upsale`, beside the button it unlocks, carrying its state on the collapsed FACE (⚠ + the reason, or ✅ + the category's name; round 43's own lesson). Three things the shape decides: the gate is `admin.settings.manage` and **not** `finance.expenses`, because spending money and naming the company's cost types are different powers — the accountant who presses pay gets a sentence saying who can choose, since a locked screen must name its key rather than read as broken (#792); `mayChoose` is passed from the SAME permission the action asks, because a door that draws what it will not let you press is not a door (#818, one screen over); and `setUpsaleCategory` re-checks the id against `expense_categories` with `active = true`, because a picker's bad value is a forged post (#506-508) and a retired category would be accepted once and then quietly stop being postable. Both doors audit under one `SETTINGS_AUDIT_ID`, moved out of the route into the settings service — two ids would split one setting's history in half. Red proofs ×2. Verified in a real browser as all three roles: the owner gets the picker and the saved name comes back on the face, the buxgalter gets the sentence, the seller sees no panel at all.

826. **The whole-module audit (his «5 etapli hisobni analiz qilib chiq — hammasini to'g'ri qurdimi»): 36 eyes over docs/VED.md, 27 candidates, 14 refuted, 13 confirmed.** Eight spec-slice finders compared every law, the tariff table cell by cell, the four dictionaries and the five phases against the code; every finding got its own skeptic told to refute it from the source, the tests and the record. What HELD is worth the sentence: the AI fences, the seal's immutability, the tariff numbers, the upsale money rules, the access scopes and the engine's honesty all survived adversarial reading. What did not is #827-828. The method note: the 14 refutations are why the method exists — among them «admin can change a sealed price was not built» (refuted: the correction path is a recorded STRICTER superset, #771) and «the six refusals are actually nine» (refuted: the audit added three and recorded them). A finding that cannot survive its own skeptic is a rumor with a file name.

827. **Two audit blockers, both in the promise's plumbing.** (a) The offer-PDF route selected the newest offer for a version with NO approval filter, so a below-floor price still WAITING for permission rendered as a customer-facing sheet to anyone with the URL — the panel hiding the link was a courtesy, not a door. `releasedOfferWhere()` is the clause's one home now (the card price and the route share it). (b) A CORRECTION sealed over a card carrying a released offer left the LOCK on the old client price while the card carried the new floor — `releasedPriceFor` was entity-keyed with no supersession clause — and `updateLead`, comparing the posted value against the lock, refused every later ✏️ save with `quote_sealed` for ever. Three parts: `offerStandsSql()` (version-set's two clauses, correlated through the offer's own version) retires a superseded promise from the card price; `releaseOffer`'s claim carries the same clause and refuses `superseded` instead of writing a dead quote onto the card; and `quoteLockedFor` decides by the CLOCK — later writer wins between the standing offer and the newest seal — because a deal carries many jobs and the card column is last-writer-wins between offers and seals. One red proof stayed GREEN on the filtered run and went red only on the full file: the strip only bites when an OLDER standing offer exists on the deal, which the filtered run never creates (#166: the fixture must provoke the geometry).

828. **Eight agreed behaviours that shipped missing or shrunk, restored.** Law 11: the bot path persisted only the AI digest — the seller's typed and forwarded TEXT lived in a 30-minute in-memory state whose sole consumer was the model, so the VED priced jobs off a summary nobody could reopen; the words go onto the note now («Sotuvchi yuborgani (asl matn)», 20 000-char cap that SAYS when it cut). Law 6: the workspace grew the «lug'atga yozish» button 0086's own comment promised — a typed rate is remembered into `calc_rates` as `source='correction'` by a person's press, never silently. Law 7: the lgota is OFFERED from the last sealed decision on the code (`lgotaLastByCode`, only decisions that carried an exemption — offering «no lgota» would nag every ordinary group; the offer is a button that applies through `setRatesAction`, so it clears the confirm like any change). Law 5: the stale ⚠ renders in the WORKSPACE beside the baza actually pricing the job, not only on the dictionary screen. Law 8: «very small cargo gets only a warning» finally has the warning — `SMALL_FREIGHT_USD = 50`, MONEY-based so the per-kg branch is under the same rule. Law 10: last FIVE quotes, not three. Law 4: `/finance/[clientId]` shows the accountant «tannarx · mijoz narxi» per quoted deal, gated on the upsale scope and NOT `finance.view` — the difference between the two numbers IS the upsale, and the VED holds `finance.manage`. And the CHANGELOG's phase-A entry was E1's body verbatim — the queue round had no owner-facing record at all; restored.

829. **The third calc door — «Hisoblatishga yuborish» from a thread (his «yaxshi, qilib bersang kerak»), built on the review's two blockers.** Select-mode taps on a bubble would fire the lightbox, the audio player or the file anchor underneath — so the island's listener is DOCUMENT-CAPTURE and, only while select mode is on, claims every click inside a `[data-msg-id]` before anything beneath can see it; off, it touches nothing. The bubbles stay plain server markup (one listener for five hundred messages, the share sheet's shape). Two steps: the first press reads (the AI on a 20 s leash — an interactive press is not the bot's answer-later chat — degrading to the manual parser), the second lands; the MATERIAL is rebuilt server-side on both, because the ids are a forged post (#514) and law 11 wants the words themselves. Refused whole on any id outside the viewer's fence — never trimmed, or the request lands silently missing material. The DEAL card passes its own deal as the target (the client's newest open deal is the bot's rule and the wrong answer when a different job is on screen); a supervision viewer's selection can span two managers, so every line is labeled and the bar says «matn kartaga yoziladi» — publishing a colleague's fenced thread onto the shared lenta is a knowing act. STATED CUTS: files are not copied onto the note (two attachment rows over one storage key would let either delete take the other's bytes; the material counts them and the VED's supervision view opens the thread), and `note_taken` on the pre-minted note id is the double-press fence.

830. **Sotuvchi samaradorligi — two FUNCTIONS with two return types, because «tannarx korinmasin sotuvchiga» (his answer) must be structural, not disciplinary.** `sellerPerformanceAll` (finance.reports) carries profit; `sellerPerformanceOwn` returns a type with NO cost-derived property and its file section may not name `profitByClient`/`costEntries`/`costAllocations` — the fence test reads the source below the OWN marker, comments stripped first (#725's lesson: my own prose was the first false match). The scope file keys 'own' on `reports.own_clients`, NOT `crm.leads` — the logist holds crm.leads and is nobody's `sales_manager_id`, a permanently empty row. The «—» cohort is a first-class row: 1,402 of 1,692 clients carried no manager on deploy day, and a roll-up keyed on manager would silently shed most of the company while reading as a complete answer. Two period vocabularies convert ONCE (`readPeriod`'s exclusive `to` for the timestamptz clock, its inclusive `dan/gacha` strings for `profitByClient`'s date columns — mixed, every month end counts a day twice). Attribution is `clients.sales_manager_id` (round 91's column) at CHARGED money — deliberately a different clock from tahlil's sellers table (leads won at QUOTED money); the subtitle names it so the owner comparing the two reads a difference, not a bug. And his sentence closed an OLD hole the audit had named: `/upsale` printed the sealed floor to the seller's own rows — both prints now stand behind the scope guard, pinned by a fence whose first version stayed green on its own red proof (a proximity window found the SELLER column's guard nearby; the oracle now removes the exact guarded forms and demands nothing survives outside them).

831. **The loading screen finally has a reverse gear (his report 1, answer B: «plandan ham chiqib skladga qaytsin»).** A scanned box could never come back off a still-loading truck — the only doors were cancelling the WHOLE batch or letting the mismatch surface at the destination as `missing_in_transit`. `removeLoadedCode` takes a box or CR- code, reverts every member that is `loading` on THIS batch to `in_stock`, clears `current_batch_id` (his answer B — off the plan too, so re-loading is a fresh scan with the not-on-plan ceremony if the plan no longer covers it), strips the flags (the batch-cancel rule: an on-spot flag must not ride into the box's next life), and writes a `load_removed` movement. ONLINE-only and two-tap on purpose, stated on the sheet: a removal is a decision made WITH the logist, not a rhythm at three boxes a second, and an offline reversal queue would have to reconcile against the load queue's unsent scans — two outboxes disagreeing about one box. Crate semantics follow the physics: a single carton taken out of a crate whose siblings stay aboard loses its `crateId` (it physically left the yashik — a whole crate coming off is scanned as the crate), a crate-code removal keeps every membership. Refused once departed — that is `resolveMissing`'s job at the other end. Membership everywhere else needed nothing: pre-departure there is no `batch_departed` movement, so the manifest, the batch register and the in-transit report already read a removed box as never having ridden.

832. **«Skladda topdim» — the stocktake's found-here rule for ONE scan (his report 2, «shunday feature qilsa bo'ladimi? trade-off xavfli buzilishlar ketmasmikan?»), and the latent bug it exposed in the stocktake itself.** `acceptFoundBox`: a box standing on this floor while the record says a truck or another warehouse moves here by one confirmed scan — reality wins, `inventory_found` movement (the stocktake's own cause, so every report treats the two paths alike), and the truck's planners are told their manifest shrank (`BoxFoundHere`). His trade-off question answered in the guards: the physical QR must be in hand; `loading` is refused (that truck is being loaded RIGHT NOW and #831 is its one writer), `issued` is refused (un-issuing is a handover decision), `void`/`lost` are refused (a recorded loss is not erased by a scan — reviving a lost box is a manager's decision, stated cut); money cannot move because tannarx membership reads `batch_departed` movements, not the live pointer; and the destination's finish does not flag it missing, because `finishUnload` flags only boxes still `in_transit`. Two-tap: the scan only looks the box up, nothing moves until the operator has read where the record had it. THE LATENT BUG: `reconcileInventory` landed every found box `in_stock` — and `resolveMissing`'s own comment says why that is wrong at a customs/distribution warehouse, which every Uzbek destination is: the box hides from every «tayyor» list for ever. Both found paths land by warehouse TYPE now; red-proven on each separately.

833. **A crushed carton has its own button (his report 3, «ha to'g'ri»).** The only door to `lost` outside a truck's missing flow was the FULL stocktake — a manager standing over one wet carton had to count the building. `markBoxLost`: receipts.void, judged at the warehouse the box STANDS in, reason mandatory, `lost` + `statusReason` + a `marked_lost` movement; the crate pointer and the plan pointer clear in the same breath (a lost member makes its crate undissolvable — the receipt-void rule — and a planned box leaves its plan). `lost`, not `void`: void is «this box never really existed», lost is «it existed and is gone», and `splitForCorrection` already treats it as terminal-and-left-alone, so void/move/return keep working around it. The MONEY deliberately does not move — whether the client's bill shrinks is the damage-discount decision on the deal, a person's call, never a warehouse button's side effect; the sentence saying so is on the success message. The client's SELLER is told (`BoxLost`): compensation is their conversation, and a loss the seller learns about from the client is worse than one they raise first.

834. **The structural lock split in two (his report 5, answer 5b).** «Birorta karobka skladdan chiqdi» froze a receipt's measures for EVERYBODY for ever — and a wrong kub/kg frozen mid-journey feeds the tannarx allocation, the truck's capacity numbers and the client's bill with a number everyone knows is false. The split: the box COUNT stays locked for everyone (a carton count is what the truck, the labels and the scans agree on — changing it mid-journey would mint or void boxes that are physically somewhere), while the MEASURES unlock for `receipts.void` holders — the same manager level that can void the receipt outright. Three consequences ride the correction: `recomputeAll` runs when the totals changed and not only when the count did (weight- and volume-based allocations read the very numbers that moved — the shrink hook's own reasoning, extended); the audit diff names the change as every lot edit always has; and the receipt's AUTHOR is told (`ReceiptMeasureCorrected`, the arrival-diff rule: the person whose record changed hears it first, never the client). Red-proven both ways: the measure gate stripped lets the author through, the count guard stripped lets a manager grow a mid-journey lot.

835. **/inventory came back to the menu (his «ha qaytar»), wearing two doors.** Hidden since the RFID note — «counting a warehouse by hand-scanning every box is not a job anyone will do» — and that reasoning still stands for the stocktake; what changed is that the screen now carries the job that IS done by hand: the single found-box accept. The warehouse pick leads to a mode chooser (bitta / to'liq), the single accept first because it is the everyday one; nav entry under `scan.load`, curated onto both warehouse menus (the fence went 7 → 8 / 10 → 11 and says why). The three new notification types joined MUTE_GROUPS.alerts — each one means the record and the floor disagreed. VERIFICATION STANCE, stated: the services are integration-proven (9 tests, red proofs ×4 by string edit), the screen→action→gate wiring is source-fenced (`warehouse-corrections-wire.test.ts`, #531's shape, comments stripped per #725), and the three new surfaces were walked in a real browser at 360×800 with screenshots; no new e2e spec — a loading-state fixture for the remove sheet would re-run half of m3 to stage one button, and the house precedent (rounds 22, 96) is to state that trade rather than hide it.

836. **Round two of the corrections, and the DESIGN REVIEW earned its place: seven lenses, 45 raw findings, 26 confirmed, and it overruled me on three of the four items.** His four reports («musorga ketdi degan narsani inventarizatsiyaga qo'sh», «hammasini qabul qilish tugmasini skladchilardan olib tashla», «10 ta karobka kelsa 10 ta sms», «qaysi sklad qanchalik to'lgan va yuk necha kun qolib ketgan») were designed, then judged BEFORE any code by seven code-grounded reviewers and a judge told to refute. Three of my four designs came back wrong in a way that would have shipped: the bin scan had no warehouse fence and would have written off cargo in another country; taking the accept-all button away would have left the operator the button that DECLARES CARGO LOST; and putting the arrival event inside the Telegram sender would have lost it for ever on five terminal exits. Two more findings were about doors I proposed to build that already existed. **The method's own lesson: a design review that only agrees is not a review.**

837. **The bin scan: a fence that lives in the caller is not a fence.** `markBoxLost` checked the box's STATUS and nothing about WHERE it stood — the location rule lived only in the receipt card's action, which loads the box and authorises at `box.currentWarehouseId`. The new door authorises at the SCREEN's warehouse and resolves the scanned code globally (an operator holds a carton, not a warehouse list), so a Yiwu scanner typing a Tashkent code would have binned it, stamped the audit row TASHKENT, and told that client's seller their goods were destroyed. `atWarehouseId` is now a REQUIRED argument (an optional one fails open, #790) and the compiler named every caller. `binCandidate` resolves-then-refuses in its own read: `box_elsewhere` points at the accept-found door, `use_unload_screen` at the unload screen, and `void`/`lost`/`issued` are terminal. **The GATE, and I overruled myself here:** the owner's sentence describes the ACT, not the actor, his own M6 answer put «who is lost» with the manager, and all three existing doors to `lost` say `receipts.void` — shipping the fourth at `scan.load` would have left the same operator refused on the tick-list one mode over and allowed five write-offs from the tile beside it. `mayWriteOffBox` is now the one home for that question, so widening it takes the stocktake's tick-list with it rather than leaving two doors on ONE screen answering differently. Told to him in plain Uzbek with the widening offered.

838. **«Xabar logistga ketadi» would have been silent for most cargo.** The whole notification block sat inside `if (about?.salesManagerId)` — and that column is NULL for unclaimed cargo and for **1,402 of 1,692 active clients**. So the owner's one stated requirement for this feature would have produced nothing, with no error and no row, for exactly the cargo most likely to be crushed and forgotten. One unconditional union now (seller ∪ `plans.manage`) through ONE `notifyStaffTelegram` call, because that function de-dupes only WITHIN a call and the logist holds `clients.manage` and is routinely somebody's sales manager — two calls would have sent that person the same sentence twice. Red-proven on a receipt whose client has no manager.

839. **The undo I was about to build already existed, three ways broken.** `setBoxStatus` has allowed `lost → in_stock` since M6, at the exact gate I proposed to invent, and `acceptFoundBox`'s «ask a manager» refusal was a POINTER at that door rather than evidence it was missing. What it actually did: landed a blanket `in_stock`, so a Tashkent carton restored at a warehouse that issues to clients never reached a «tayyor» list and the cabinet said «O'zbekistonda» for ever; asked NOTHING about the receipt, so a box lost before its prixod was voided came back as live, plannable, sellable stock hanging off a receipt that officially never happened (#723's shape, one door over — and `stockByWarehouse` filters on box status alone, so it would have shown in /stock and in the plan picker); and left `current_batch_id` pointing at the truck it went missing from. Three red proofs, one per rule. `box-state.ts`'s «never coming back to a shelf» comment was rewritten rather than left to disagree with the code.

840. **Item 2, where the review beat the design outright: raising the gate on the SAFE button leaves the operator the LOSSY one.** The owner asked for «hammasini qabul qilish» to leave the skladchi — he wants the cartons scanned. But 🏁 «Tushirish tugadi» had **no permission gate in the component at all**, and closing over outstanding cartons flags every one `missing_in_transit`, which then REFUSES the client's handover until a manager resolves each. A 150-box truck at Tashkent at 19:00 with only an operator present would have gone from «one tap accepts it» to «one tap loses it» — verbatim the production incident that created the accept-all button. So both shortcuts moved together: 📥 accept-all and 🏁 finish-WITH-REMAINING are `receipts.void` at the destination now, the plain finish (nothing outstanding) stays the unloader's own, and the SERVICE refuses too (#531) with `opts.mayCloseWithMissing` defaulting FALSE so a caller that forgets the question gets the safe answer. The operator gets a sentence telling them to scan the rest, and `finishConfirm` in all four locales stopped naming a button they no longer have. Also fixed in the same edit: `canResolve` was `permissions.has('receipts.void')` with NO warehouse test while its action authorises at the destination — and that `authorize` was OUTSIDE the try/catch, so the AuthError escaped into an async `onClick` with no boundary and the operator saw nothing at all. Every action in that file answers in words now.

841. **The lower-gated bypass, closed before it became the path of least resistance.** `acceptFoundBox` is `scan.load` and refused only void/lost/issued/loading — so an operator denied the accept-all shortcut could have scanned the whole manifest into /inventory instead, one tap each, through a WEAKER gate, and the records would have degraded with it: `finishUnload` counts only `unload_scan`/`undocumented_transfer`, so the logist's summary would read «Qabul qilindi: 0», no arrival notice would be claimed and no seller told. A box riding a truck bound HERE and still in transit now refuses with `use_unload_screen`. The genuine stray — a lorry heading somewhere else, a box recorded at another warehouse — is untouched, and both the found-here resolution and the found-box accept now CLAIM the arrival notice, which neither did.

842. **«10 ta karobka kelsa 10 ta sms»: the staff event moved onto the claim, and the design's first version would have thrown it away.** Round 98 fixed the CUSTOMER's copy with `client_notices` and left the event on the per-scan path «for the staff side, which is what it was written for» — his seller was getting one Telegram per carton. Moving it looked like a two-line change and was not. `ReadyForPickup` is ALSO the deal's `ready` cargo trigger and one of ten curated automation triggers, and `sendDueArrivalNotices` has **five terminal exits before it ever sends** (no bot token, client gone, batch gone, nothing landed, no linked chat), each settling the row out of a queue that only ever re-reads `pending`. Hanging the event off that path would have lost the seller's message, the funnel move and the rules PERMANENTLY for every customer who has never opened the bot — and for every truck unloaded while the burned bot token was being rotated. So: its own selector (`staff_notified_at IS NULL`, whatever `status` says), swept BEFORE the token check; its own fence and the emit in ONE transaction (claim-then-emit as two statements is at-most-once with a restart in the middle and no queue row to show for it); and `claimed_by` on the row, because the event used to carry the scanner as its actor and an automation rule assigning «to whoever did it» would otherwise find nobody and stop firing with its own `fire_count` not even moving. Migration **0090**; ledger must reach **91**.

843. **Two live defects found on the way, both in the customer's own path.** (a) `arrivedSummary`'s membership was `batch_departed` alone — so a box AUTO-TRANSFERRED at the destination (the reality-wins path: it rode the truck and the plan never knew) produced no summary at all, and the customer was never told about a delivery the seller had already been told about. Both halves of the membership are one predicate now. (b) The client drain had **no claim**: a plain `SELECT … status='pending'`, and pg-boss re-dispatches a slow job while the first is still running — two sweeps, same rows, the customer told twice, which is the exact noise this table exists to prevent. 0082's shape (`FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`, ten-minute reclaim) plus an `AbortSignal.timeout` on the Telegram call, round 101's lesson one module over. And the token exit stopped settling `skipped`: with no token the sweep now simply returns, as `sendPendingTelegram` has always done, instead of silently burying every message queued during a rotation.

844. **A truck unloaded over two days is TWO waves and one claim row.** The unique index makes the second morning's claim a no-op, so without a re-arm neither the customer nor the seller is ever told about wave two — and the event they did get described a quarter of the delivery. `rearmArrivalNotice` fires only when the claim was REFUSED and only on a row that has already spoken (a notice still pending will read the new totals when its own window closes), clearing the staff fence and putting the Telegram row back to pending with a fresh window. Red-proven with a second scanning session.

845. **The fill bar already existed, had never once rendered, and the age clock as designed would have reset itself.** `warehouseFill` has been in `reports/queries.ts` and drawn on /dashboard since M6 — writing «one grouped query beside stockByWarehouse» would have minted a second home already guaranteed to disagree (one filters `active`, the other does not). It never rendered because it filtered `isNotNull(capacity_m3)` and **all nine of his warehouses have a NULL capacity** — measured. So: capacity-less warehouses are rows with NO bar and a link to the form, empty warehouses still appear (a card that hides «bo'sh» cannot be told from a broken one), and the age is the movement that LANDED the box, `landedHereSql` — the agent sheet's own three clauses, now exported from their one home, plus `found_at_origin` which passes all three while describing a journey that did not happen (latent in the sheet too). Trusting `to_warehouse_id` alone would have reset the age to zero the morning a logist plans the carton onto a truck, i.e. been most wrong about the cargo somebody is already arguing about — the third time this exact rule has had to be restated (#513, and the E1 audit's «#513 inside the round about #513»). No `confirmed_at` fallback: a receipt writes a movement with a NULL from-warehouse, so `IS DISTINCT FROM` already covers cargo received here and never moved — and 4 of 6 confirmed receipts in `gsr_dev` have a NULL `confirmed_at` anyway. The oldest day travels with a COUNT past `stale_stock_days`, because one forgotten carton makes a bare maximum a permanent red number nobody reads; the label says «shu skladda» because three other surfaces answer the same question from the receipt date and for cargo that crossed a border the two differ by the whole China→Uzbekistan leg. `agingSummary` was widened from `in_stock` to all four in-warehouse statuses in the same edit — every Uzbek warehouse lands cargo `ready_for_pickup`, so the aging report was blind to exactly the uncollected cargo it is read for. **STATED TO HIM: the bars stay empty until he types the nine capacities.**

846. **The Kashgar «нет связи» was, on the best measured evidence, an advertisement for a door that does not exist.** His screenshot: the page renders (the title is on screen, so the server was reached) and only the box-list fetch dies, on every plan screen, with the internet working. Caddy adds `Alt-Svc: h3=":443"` to every response by default, and this compose publishes 443 over **TCP only** — round 110 recorded exactly that mismatch as stated-not-done. A browser that believes the header moves its NEXT requests to QUIC/UDP, where nothing answers: first connection fine, everything after it dead — the report's exact shape, and Chinese networks interfere with QUIC anyway, which is why it surfaced in Kashgar. Measured with a real Caddy 2.8.4: default config sends the header, `servers { protocols h1 h2 }` does not, gzip intact, both generated Caddyfiles validate. Advertising only what is served beats publishing UDP 443 — TCP h2 is the transport this whole system demonstrably works over. HONESTY RECORDED: the header predates his deploy, so this is the strongest candidate and not a proof; the alternatives (round 110's service-worker change cached on the phone, a server 500 the old wording hid, plain network interference) were told to him with the two discriminating tests (incognito, Wi-Fi vs LTE). Which is the second half of the fix: the scan screens' one sentence said «нет связи» for BOTH «never reached the server» and «the server answered 500» — the sentence on the screen is the diagnosis a phone photo carries, so it now names the class (`scanSnapshotServer` with the status code) and the next screenshot answers the question this one could not. The inventory screen, which had no catch at all and sat on «Yuklanmoqda…» for ever, got the same card.

847. **The annul is voidReceipt's bigger brother, and the design review inverted its spine before a line shipped.** The owner's ask (2026-08-26): test data reached production and went through the WHOLE flow — «anulirovat qilish mumkin bo'lsin … barcha finance rasxodlari ham unga tegishli tozalanishi kerak, anulirovat qilingan yuklarni ro'yxati tursin». The design was judged by six code-grounded lenses and 25 objections were CONFIRMED (0 refuted) — five of them blockers, and the first was my own cascade order: v1 committed the receipt's money voids as separate transactions BEFORE a refusable cargo step, so a refused annul (one planned box, one fat-fingered receipt) would have irreversibly destroyed a live receipt's costs and partner debts with no undo anywhere in the codebase — «money first» (#288) is a REFUSAL, never a license to auto-void ahead of a refusal. Shipped shape: ONE transaction — the guards (role, reason, planned/loading boxes, pending_agent plans naming the lots, the C6 verdict-killer), `voidCostEntryInTx` per live receipt-scope entry (the #529 pairing on the caller's tx), the box flip through the ONE terminal writer `void-box.ts` (voidReceipt uses it too, #513; `lost` boxes keep their written reason), crates the annul emptied dissolved, batches left with zero live members while in_transit/arrived retired to `cancelled` with their driver devices revoked, the receipt stamp LAST. Gate = the super_admin ROLE in one predicate (`mayAnnul`) checked in the action AND the service (#531) — no permission can separate super_admin from admin (both hold ALL) and #170 forbids minting one; the registry page reads on `admin.audit.browse` so the hub tile never teases a role the page bounces.

848. **The aftermath is re-runnable, and the re-press is the repair.** Redistribution of a shared truck's costs cannot live in the annul's transaction (the lot-edit rule: money must not roll back the cargo fix), so a crash between the stamp and the recompute must not be permanent — and v1's `already_voided` refusal made it exactly that, for ever, behind the only door that knew what to recompute. Now an annul pressed on an already-annulled receipt runs the aftermath again instead of refusing: recomputeAll per ridden batch, recomputeEntry per packed-crate entry, then the EMPTY-SCOPE sweep — a live batch/crate entry whose scope (judged on `scopeBoxIds`, never on the allocation count, because a missing FX rate also means zero allocations) came back empty is voided with its partner charge, which is the all-test truck's own freight: without it that money stood in the P&L for ever, allocated to nothing, while entry-based and allocation-based reports permanently disagreed about the same dollar. The in-tx audit row lists the recompute as PENDING; the aftermath writes its own when it lands.

849. **`ne(status,'void')` on the batch and crate allocation bases is a RECORDED correction, not a no-op — my scout's claim was false.** The design said «no existing door produces a void box in those bases»; the regression lens proved two do it today: voidReceipt on crated shelf boxes (clears crateId, the crate_packed movement stays) and a hub-relanded box (internal CN leg lands `in_stock` again) later voided by shrink or setBoxStatus, with its batch_departed movement intact. So completing #530 — a void box is a counting mistake and must not carry shares ANYWHERE — reprices those old crates/trucks on their next recompute (an FX edit, a departure). Deliberate and stated to the owner: those shares belonged to phantom boxes, and the silent alternative was worse — they were already repriced wrongly the day the box was voided under the old rule.

850. **«A void box is not cargo» became a CLOSED SET, because the fence found the rule hand-written in ten places and missing from six.** The completeness lens listed the movement-aggregate surfaces the design's first sweep missed — the batch pricing screen the accountant prices the truck from, the client card's trips block, the map popup's contents, the /batches and /trucks boards, the arrivals «trucks heading here», the bot's batch answer (which joins no boxes at all and needed a NOT EXISTS) — all FIGURES about a truck. All eleven now carry the exclusion, and `annul-wire.test.ts` closes the set the tx-pool way: every file in src/ touching `batch_departed` must be in the FIXED list (figures, exclusion required and asserted) or the HISTORICAL list (manifest/customs documents, scanners, membership resolvers, status-filtered cabinet reads — history keeps what rode, round 92's rule), and a new consumer goes red until it chooses. The bot's «oxirgi prixod» also learned `status='confirmed'` — voidReceipt keeps confirmed_at, so an annulled prixod stayed the client's newest for ever.

851. **The users form could hand ANY admin the super_admin role, so the annul's gate was one form post away from theater.** The grants editor has refused self-widening since its birth («you cannot grant what you do not hold» — roles.ts) but the ASSIGNMENT door never did: resolveRoleIds accepts every role code, updateUserAction has no escalation rule, and until now the role-keyed powers were reads, so nobody paid. The annul is the first role-keyed DESTRUCTIVE write; both users actions now refuse adding OR removing super_admin unless the actor holds it (`super_admin_locked`, worded on the form) — removing gated too, because locking the owner out is the same post in the other direction. Red-proven.

852. **Client money is counted honestly and voided by a person — the annul never touches revenue.** A charge names no receipt (only clientId/dealId/batchId) and a payment only its kassa, so «the receipt's charge» is a heuristic, and the product lens showed the v1 candidate count answering 0 exactly when a test PAYMENT still inflated a real cash box. The preview and the registry now count ALL of the client's live rows and link to the ledger, where voidTransaction already exists with its own gate — the registry's money column drains to 0 as he cleans, which makes it the done/not-done checklist, and over-counting on a mixed client is safe because it is a link and a number, never an auto-void. The bulk tool (registry → client code → tick → one reason → one press) exists because his real case is DOZENS of receipts, and a per-card button was no cleanup workflow at all; deactivating the test client afterwards is a stated hint, never automated.

853. **«Qaysi partiyada kelgan» reached the shelves and the plan, by READING round 92's rule instead of restating it.** The owner's ask (2026-08-29, while cleaning test data): the stock view and the planning screens must say which truck brought each row. The rule already had one home — `documents/arrivals.ts`, whose comment block records why every clause is load-bearing — and `arrivalsForLots` was already measured at 57 ms on his fullest warehouse, so the round is three surfaces and one wrapper. The wrapper (`arrivalCodesForPairs`) exists because the STOCK table's grain is (lot, warehouse) and a lot standing in two places arrived on two different answers: grouped per distinct warehouse (bounded by his nine), one arrivals query each, keyed on the PAIR — red-proven by fixing the key to one warehouse and watching the truck print on the shelf it never left. Surfaces: /stock gets a `partiya` column (in STOCK_COLUMNS as data, so the chooser, the saved views and the XLSX inherit it; `L.batch` already existed in the report labels); the plan editor's stock feed (`/api/plans/stock`) carries `arrival` on the loose lots AND the crates (a Kashgar crate's members rode real trucks — the codes are the distinct union over its member lots), drawn as a small `🚚 KA-29` line under the code, not a column — that table already fights for its width, and an emoji+code needs no translation; the plan VIEW page reads the same answer per line, so the logist on screen and the agent's XLSX group by the same fact. TEST LESSON, #166 again: the first editor-feed red proof stayed GREEN because `arrival:` also names the crate half — the wire assertion now counts both occurrences.

854. **VED 2.0 phase 1 — the duty stopped being one number, because PP-3818 prices in four shapes.** His two files (the lex.uz PP-3818 export and the 2026 hisoblash qo'llanma) are the spec now, and the first thing they prove is that `duty_pct` alone cannot hold the law: 198 rows read «X %, lekin T AQSH dollaridan kam emas» (MAX of the two halves), 41 vehicle rows read «X % + T USD/sm³» (their SUM), and a schema that keeps the 20 and drops the $3/dona floor mis-prices exactly the light goods this company ships — on the guide's own kurtka example the floor IS the duty ($3,000 vs $2,700). Migration **0091** (ledger must reach **92**): `duty_mode/duty_specific/duty_unit` on `calc_rates` AND on the `calc_groups` snapshot — the group's copy so a sealed MAX price goes on explaining its own floor after the dictionary moves. On the group the mode is stored NULL for advalor, so every group sealed before 0091 keeps meaning exactly what it meant; the pair CHECK is TWO-directional (`(mode='advalor') = (specific IS NULL)` AND the same for the unit), because one direction alone lets a 'max' row lose its floor and quietly become the plain percentage it was created to beat.

855. **The whole decree seeded, and idempotence lives on the ROW, not on «is the table empty».** «Shuni baza qilib ol stavkalarni» — 1,489 rows parsed out of his lex.uz export (1,250 advalor / 198 max / 41 plus; codes at the law's own grains, 4 to 10 digits; zero parse failures over three iterations, judge-hardened) into `pp3818-rates.json`, source `'pp3818'` so a person's later correction is tellable from the law's own text. NOT the freight seed's «only into an empty table» rule: the VED types corrections into this same table, so «empty» stops being true on day two and a redeploy could never repair a partial seed. Instead the seed's date is a CONSTANT — every run mints the same (code, date) pairs and `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` leaves both an earlier run and every person's row untouched; measured, second run inserts 0. Two numbers in it were judge blockers: `vat_pct` is **12** on every row (the flat law rate — `pullRates` copies the dictionary verbatim, so a seed left at the column default 0 would have silently priced every calculation VAT-free), and `fee_usd` is **0** because the fee is not a per-code fact at all (see #858). `pp3818-seed.test.ts` pins the counts, the mode split, the 598 zero-percent rows and eight hand-checked rows against the DOCUMENT, so a parser re-run that moves any of them is a question, never noise.

856. **The lookup takes the LONGEST stored prefix, and a correction CARRIES the law's shape forward.** PP-3818 writes 1,228 four-digit headings with 10-digit exceptions carved out, so «6403120000» must find its own 5 % row while «6403520000», which has no row, falls back to heading 6403's «20 %, min $3/juft» — the walk is longest-prefix-first, newest date within the winning prefix, exactly as every dictionary here reads. `saveRates` grew the matching rule (J11): a person correcting the PERCENTAGE of a MAX code and saying nothing about the shape keeps the $2/kg floor — otherwise the first hand correction silently degrades the code to advalor — and passing `'advalor'` explicitly IS how the shape is removed. The carry reads only an EXACT-code standing row: heading 6403's shape must not ride onto a 10-digit exception a person is minting on purpose. Red-proven by reversing the walk to shortest-first (two tests red, unit and integration).

857. **The additional duty (BK 300-1, 28.02.2026) is the certificate's price, its bands are inclusive-HIGH, and a lgota kills it.** Without an origin certificate: +5/10/15/20 % of BQ by the code's ADVALOR band. The law's text («10 dan 20 gacha») does not decide the boundaries and the guide says to ask the post — but the guide's own example 9.2 prices a 20 % code at +15 %, so exactly-20 lands in the HIGHER band and the engine follows the example until the post disagrees (my first draft had it the other way; the judge caught it against the guide's own arithmetic). The 598 zero-percent rows still pay +5 % without a certificate — that is the law's <10 band, not a bug — and the breakdown carries `addDutyPct` so the screen can say so. A duty-FREE lgota zeroes the additional duty too: a lgota is proven origin, and origin proven and origin unknown cannot both be true of one consignment. `has_certificate` defaults TRUE on the request (his answer: «ha sertifikat bor deb qoy») and is overridable per GROUP, because a sborniy truck mixes senders (J21). VAT's base grew the whole chain: (BQ + boj + qo'shimcha boj + aksiz) × 12 %.

858. **The customs fee prices a DECLARATION, lands INSIDE the sealed customs figure, and its million-dollar boundary was caught by its own test before it shipped.** VMQ-55's scale is a BHM coefficient by the declaration's value in USD — so it cannot be a per-code dictionary number (a three-group job would pay it three times; the pull now leaves the group's fee EMPTY) and cannot be a separate section (E1's `moneyPct` is two measurements of ONE payable-to-the-state fact, and the fee is part of that fact). `customsFeeFor` computes it once per request over the summed group values and folds it into `customs_usd`, with the receipt (`coefficient BHM ≈ $x`) in the breakdown and on the screen. The scale is written in so'm, so the day's UZS rate is load-bearing: read WITHOUT `rateFor`'s earliest-row fallback — this module's rule is that a missing rate is a refusal (`fee_fx_missing`, a seal blocker with a sentence), never an invented conversion. `fee_override_usd` on the request is the VED's word that the declaration count or tier differs; the −20 % preliminary-declaration discount is stated, not modelled — it lives in the override. THE CATCH: the tiers are «gacha»-inclusive going down but «1 000 000 va undan ortiq → 25» starts AT the million — my shipped comparison gave exactly $1M the 20 it does not get, and the boundary-pinning test went red against the real code before the commit. A boundary table's test earns its place by disagreeing once.

859. **The engine prices kg, dona and 1000_dona, and REFUSES the other four units rather than reinterpreting the quantity.** A calc request's items carry weight and a piece count and nothing else; a litre, a pair, a cm³ or a m² exists on no item, and reading a juft rate against a piece count is a number that is simply wrong (J16/J20 — my draft had exactly that reinterpretation). `unit_unsupported` is a refusal with words («bojni qo'lda kiriting»), the dictionary still STORES all seven units because the law names all seven and a seed that dropped litr rows would answer «no rate» about codes the law prices, and `1000_dona` divides — 4,000 pieces is 4 units, not 4,000 (the test pins the ÷1000, the mistake that is off by three orders of magnitude). The 21 clause-cut vehicle rows are all sm³ and refuse through the same gate.

860. **The seed flipped `rate_off_dictionary`'s world from empty to full, so the warning finally learned to compare VALUES.** The predicate was «the dictionary answered AND the source is typed» — its own comment claimed «a person typed a DIFFERENT one» but no comparison existed, which was invisible while the dictionaries shipped empty and became «name every group in the company» the day 0091 seeded 1,489 rows (the exact failure #767's `dictionaryRates !== null` clause was built against, from the other side). Now it fires only when the typed duty or VAT differs from the book's — typing the law's own numbers deviates from nothing. Same deploy-shock class, same fix shape as the lugatlar screen: `listRates` unfiltered now shows only person-entered rows and a typed code searches the WHOLE book both ways (the heading AND its carved-out exceptions — the pair the lookup weighs), because rendering 1,489 rows is /stock's DOM crush (round 68) on the one screen the VED opens daily. And the certificate chip's flip unconfirms exactly the groups that INHERIT the request's answer — a group that answered for itself keeps its numbers and its ✅ (red-proven by widening the scope and watching the own-answer group lose a confirmation about numbers that never moved).

861. **VED 2.0 phase 2 — the group ceremony is deleted, and one save carries a hundred cells.** The owner's sentence this phase exists for: «gruh yasab ulangan tovarlarni ulash juda ish ko'p» — the old flow was createGroup, name it, open its fold, type the code, type the rates, move every item through a picker, then the baza per item: eight presses before a number existed. Now the table IS the workspace: the VED types a TNVED code on the ITEM row and the item lands in that code's group by itself (find-or-create, first-by-seq wins — one code, one group), with the PP-3818 rates pulled from the dictionary AT MINT (`rate_source='dictionary'`, grey on screen until a person types over them). The save is the receipt-cost-grid shape at 100× scale: drafts in client state, ONE «Saqlash» posts every changed cell — per-cell round trips at that size is round 108's freeze — into ONE transaction opened with `SELECT … FOR UPDATE` on the request row, because the judge proved `assertOpen` is a check and not a lock: two saves minting seqs off the same `max(seq)` collide on the unique index, two adds each pass the 1000 cap reading the same count, and the emptied-group DELETE races the move that just refilled it. `item_count` is recomputed as `count(*)` inside the same tx (the cached column under concurrency is how the queue label lies), the cap is judged against that count, and a live `ai_proposal_started_at` claim refuses the save (`applyProposal` deletes and recreates every group — a table edit landing mid-pass would be silently destroyed) with a ten-minute staleness window so a crashed pass cannot brick the table for ever.

862. **THE SWEEP — the judge's blocker that saved the phase from missing its own point.** `openCalcRequest` fills item codes from the TNVED memory at intake, so the commonest real request — a repeat client's — arrives with every code FILLED and every item ungrouped, and a design that groups only on *edited* cells offers that request nothing: no cell is dirty, Saqlash no-ops, and the ungrouped blocker stands for ever. The fix is one rule: EVERY save also runs the same find-or-create over every coded-but-ungrouped item in the request, so the first press heals the whole backlog («N tovar guruhlandi» in the bar), the paste path and `addItems`' memory-filled codes are covered by the same sentence, and the Saqlash button stays live at zero dirty exactly when such items exist. Red-proven: sweep stripped → the prefilled-request test red, 6 tests total.

863. **The dirty gate: nothing blesses numbers the server has never seen.** The sticky bar puts ✅, confirm-all, the certificate and (via `SealPanel`) Muhrlash beside a table full of client-side drafts — and every one of those actions reads the DATABASE. A seal pressed over forty unsaved rows locks a client-facing price that excludes them, silently. So while `dirtyCount > 0` every non-Saqlash mutating control is replaced by «Avval saqlang (N)» and the seal button waits with its own sentence; `beforeunload` guards an hour of typing against a mis-tap; and Enter on the last row grows the table (the «sometimes 1 piece» trickle must not need the mouse). A refused save keeps every typed input AND names its row — `CalcError` grew an optional `seq`, because one bare code over a 100-row save is the owner's «mujmal» complaint rebuilt at scale.

864. **The group baza is addressed by CODE, guarded by what the screen SAW — and its red proof stayed green first.** One baza cell per group is the owner's own model («bitta tnved kod uchun narx bir xil»), and addressing the fanout by code instead of group id kills two birds: a save that types a code AND its baza in one press works (the group's id does not exist when the client posts), and the hand-posted-foreign-groupId teleport the judge found dies with the id — a code cannot name another request's group. The fanout writes every member's baza as 'typed' over POST-edit membership re-read in the tx, but only after the stale fence passes: each entry carries `sawBazaUsd`/`sawMixed` — what the screen showed — and the server refuses `stale_baza` when the members moved underneath, because a browser opened before a colleague set law-5 per-item bazas must not flatten them unseen (a screen that saw the mix confirms through `window.confirm` naming the count and the values). #166's entry: the both-ends-unconfirm red proof PASSED with the losing side stripped, because the fixture's losing group emptied and was deleted before anything could ask about its ✅ — the strengthened fixture keeps a member on the losing side, and only then did the strip go red.

865. **A typo'd code mints a plausible-looking group, so the mint is LOUD and the shape is checked.** The prefix lookup is phase 1's feature and phase 2's hazard: a mistyped 10-digit code that still matches a 4-digit heading arrives with legitimate grey rates and a customs figure, and the only backstop is the ✅ E1's own record says gets rubber-stamped. Three fences: the cell and the server both refuse a code that is not 4-10 digits (`bad_code`, by row — 9-digit codes are real, the seed has six); the save's answer NAMES freshly minted groups in the bar («+N yangi guruh: 8528…»), so a typo surfaces as a surprise one-item group instead of pricing silently; and a native `<datalist>` of the request's own codes makes every repeat two digits and a pick — 100 items over 5 codes must not cost a thousand keystrokes (the judge's arithmetic against my own design). A code the book has never heard of mints with NULL rates and NULL source — 'dictionary' over nulls is a provenance lie the warnings and E1's counters would read as the book's word — and the `rates_missing` blocker stands.

866. **The layout left CardCols, and the round's own screenshot caught the cascade.** The two-column card layout left the working surface ~490 px at 1280 (measured in the scout pass) — no home for a seven-column grid — so the request page is a vertical flow now: facts strip (checklist chips intact), the seller's materials as a `<details>` whose testid rides the ROOT (a closed fold's summary is visible, its content is not — m9zo asserts visibility), the full-width table, then the panels. `calc-items` is a one-element contract: the workspace table carries it when it renders, and the read-only fallback — which is ALSO the isServerBehind degradation and the yolkira/closed rendering — carries it everywhere else, never both (strict-mode locators). The phone is read-only with the confirm and seal still live, and the sentence enumerates what works rather than reading as «this screen is dead». And the round's own 360-px screenshot caught `.btn` beating a bare `hidden` — the house classes are defined AFTER the utilities, so their display wins at equal specificity (#419's family, fourth costume): the desktop-only bar buttons hide behind a wrapper `<span className="hidden md:contents">` now, which owns no display of its own.

867. **`baza_off_dictionary` learned the values-differ half — the same correction the rates warning got in phase 1, one dictionary over.** The group-baza fanout stamps `bazaSource='typed'` on every member, and the warning's old predicate fired on SOURCE alone — so the day the baza dictionary has answers, every group in the company would carry the warning, every ✅ would record it, and E1's warned-share would be 100 % noise («a list that names everything names nothing», verbatim the defect the rates half was fixed for, now on the other dictionary). It fires only when the typed number differs from the book's — and the BASIS is part of the price: the book's own $12 per the wrong measure ($12/kg vs $12/unit) still warns. `WarningGroupFacts.items` carries the values now; the one caller had all four in scope.

868. **VED 2.0 phase 3 — the CODE says the measure, and each of the seven units keeps its one home.** The owner's design, verbatim: «tenved kodni olchov birligiga qarab kg dona m2 chiqsin va bazani shu yerda sorasin» — the law names seven pricing units and phase 1 could only read three of them off the item's own columns. Migration **0092** (ledger must reach **93**): `calc_request_items.measure_unit` + `measure_qty`, and the CHECK admits ONLY `('juft','litr','m2','sm3')` — kg stays on `weight_kg` and dona/1000_dona stay on `quantity`, because a second writable home for a fact the row already carries is two numbers that can disagree about one cargo. The pair travels together (`(measure_unit IS NULL) = (measure_qty IS NULL)`), the engine resolves EVERY unit through one `itemMeasure(item, unit)` — the value loop and the specific-duty half read the same answer — and `unit_unsupported` is RETIRED: the refusal is `measure_missing` naming the item, because «the engine cannot price m²» stopped being true and «this row has not said how many m² arrived» is the sentence the VED can act on. Both baza-basis CHECKs widened to `('unit','kg','juft','litr','m2')` — deliberately NO sm³: the 41 vehicle rows price duty per cm³ of engine, but nobody VALUES a car by displacement — a vehicle's baza is per dona, and a basis list that admits sm³ invites exactly that mistake.

869. **The revision clock — the shipped-audit's confirmed finding closed, and the proof's own instrument had to be debugged first.** The audit proved the seal computes on POOL reads and CASes on `completed_at` alone, so a save committing between the compute and the seal's transaction sealed the PRE-save snapshot. Now `calc_requests.rev` is an integer every mutator bumps under `FOR UPDATE` — ONE protocol (`mutateRequest` = lock → work → bump; `lockRequestInTx` for the doors that open their own tx) with a unit fence (`calc-clock.test.ts`) that classifies every exported workspace function as mutator-or-exempt, so a new door cannot ship unclassified. The seal captures `workspace.rev` (loadWorkspace's FIRST query) and refuses `conflict` when the in-tx re-read differs; `confirmGroup`/`confirmAllGroups` capture it through `warningsNow` — a ✅ blesses what stood on the SCREEN, and a save landing mid-press must invalidate the blessing. The deterministic proof is round 103's shape (holder connection takes the row FOR UPDATE first, the change lands as raw SQL inside the holder's own tx), and its observer was wrong TWICE before it worked: a row-lock waiter holds its tuple lock GRANTED and queues on the holder's TRANSACTIONID lock — `pg_locks.relation` is NULL, so filtering on the table name matches nothing; and `pg_stat_activity` polled through the HOLDER re-reads its own pre-block snapshot for ever, because the view freezes inside an open transaction. The working observer polls `wait_event_type='Lock'` through the POOL. Both worth remembering: the obvious query for «is he blocked?» answers about the wrong lock, from the wrong connection.

870. **`saveTable` became the ONE writer, the block became the GROUP, and the baza moved onto the ROW — the owner's own 1a.** His answer: same code with differently-priced goods = separate rows, «hech qanday guruhlash bolmasa ham boladi buni ved hodimini ozi qilib oladi» — so the group ceremony's last visible remains (the group-row baza cell, the fold's move/delete doors) are gone, the block is drawn by membership alone with a BlockFooter carrying code · law · value · LIVE customs · ✅ · ⚙, and the per-row Baza column is the price. That kills phase 2's `TableGroupBaza` fanout AND its `stale_baza`/`sawMixed` fence whole — a per-row write needs no «what did the screen see» guard, because it overwrites nobody else's row. The measure pass is one writer inside the save's tx, AFTER regroup/sweep/merge, so it sees final membership: a row entering an extended-unit block keeps a matching pair, a recode m²→kg CLEARS the pair and the answer NAMES the rows (`measuresCleared`), a recode m²→juft drops and names likewise — a 200 that meant m² must never survive as a 200 that reads juft. Legacy same-code duplicate groups merge on save ONLY when their rates are identical (`sameGroupRates`) and the merge is announced; differing rates stand — silently averaging or preferring one is a price nobody typed.

871. **Drafts are keyed by item ID, die with their row, and hold until the server's answer LANDS.** The audit's third finding, proven in the browser: a draft keyed by seq survived its row's deletion, every later save refused `not_found` about a row no longer on screen, and the dirty gate latched until a full reload — the wedge. Now the key is the item's id, the delete's success handler clears exactly that key (the m9zr red proof strips this one line and the e2e goes red at `calc-unsaved`), and clearing-after-save waits for `workspace.rev` to MOVE (`clearAfterRev`) rather than for the action to return — the action resolves before the refreshed page paints, and clearing on resolve snapped the live figures back to pre-save numbers for the length of a round trip. The clear itself is a render-time adjustment, not an effect: the React-compiler lint refused `setState` in an effect, and the render-phase form is also correct — the frame that brings the moved rev must not paint once with stale drafts. BUILD TRAP, new: Next's build lint answers from `.next/cache/eslint` and served the PRE-edit file's diagnostics twice after the fix was on disk — `rm -rf .next/cache/eslint` before believing a build that contradicts the CLI's own `pnpm lint`.

872. **The live figure is the ENGINE in the browser, and the gated assembly is shared — no partial sums anywhere.** «kirgizganda yonida rastamojka summasi chiqadgan qilsachi» (his 3a: per-row live + always-visible total): the pure engine already runs anywhere, so the table computes each block's customs from the DRAFTS as typed (`liveCustomsByGroup`) and the bar's total through `requestCustomsFor` — the same request-grain assembly `loadWorkspace` uses, extracted precisely so the fee-only-when-every-group-prices rule and the no-partial-sums rule cannot fork between server and screen. While anything is dirty the bar says «jonli» and `TotalsPanel` greys under `calc-totals-stale` — a live number and a saved number on one screen must say which is which. A refused row prints ⚠ + the reason in its own footer, never $0 (law 6, unchanged).

873. **`rate_noted` — a clause-cut rate prices WITH a visible flag, and #430 was broken a FOURTH time on this round's own cleanup.** The 21 vehicle rows whose «+ $x/sm³» clause the seed could not model price by their advalor half alone; pricing silently under a shortened law is a wrong number with provenance, so the seed folds the raw clause into the note («⚠ shart qisqargan: …»), the footer renders the chip, the warning fires on `dictionaryNote`, and a ✅ records it like every other warning. THE PROCESS FAILURE: undoing a red proof I typed `git checkout -- workspace.ts 2>/dev/null` — the reflex #430 exists to forbid, inside a one-liner whose `2>/dev/null` even hid the evidence — and it destroyed ~25 uncommitted edits of the round's largest file. Replayed verbatim from the conversation record, verified by typecheck + the full targeted suite, and the round's work was committed IMMEDIATELY after. The rule grows its missing half: commit the surgery before the proofs, and a cleanup command that names a file you have uncommitted work in is not a cleanup.
